**Supporting interpretation.** The decision is mostly right but the reliance condition must be tightened: to get the 'ordinarily good faith' special rule, the sponsor must receive the written advice from the qualified tax practitioner (for example a practitioner letter addressed to or extending reliance to the sponsor, as repository models do per subscriber). Authorization by a non-practitioner repository or forwarding by another grantor gives only the general facts-and-circumstances standard.

**Competing interpretation.** A sponsor could rely on shared advice under the general good-faith rule when it knows the practitioner and the sharing grantor's diligence (TD 9740 preamble example).

**Applicability to Unbound.** EquivalencyDetermination record must distinguish 'received from practitioner' versus 'shared by grantor/repository' and route the latter to sponsor review.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsors subscribe to a repository whose practitioners issue advice per subscriber

**Recommended answer.** Revise: the routine (non-ER) path is allowed only when the sponsor has current written advice received from a qualified tax practitioner (directly or via a repository whose practitioner issues the advice or an express reliance letter to the sponsor), no contrary knowledge, and currency computed per §53.4945-5(a)(5). Advice merely forwarded by another grantor or authorized only by a non-practitioner repository goes to sponsor review under the general good-faith standard, not the automatic path. Currency is computed from the grantee's taxable year.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Exact contractual form of repository reliance (not verified by fetch); final §4966 rules might change details.

**Consequences if wrong.** Grant treated as to a non-§170(b)(1)(A) organization without ER: 20% sponsor tax, 5% knowing managers.

**Conservative fallback.** Per-sponsor advice only.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Use a domestic intermediary (catalogued) for foreign grantees lacking practitioner-issued advice: no ED cost; donor intent preserved via non-binding preference.

**External authorization required.** Repository subscription contract under which a qualified tax practitioner issues the written advice (or a reliance letter) to the sponsor.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given an ED whose source is 'shared by another grantor' without practitioner-issued reliance to the sponsor, When approval is requested, Then the routine path is refused and a SponsorReview task is created.
- Given practitioner advice issued to the sponsor dated 2026-03-01 based on FY2025 calendar-year data, When release occurs 2027-12-30, Then release proceeds; on 2028-01-02 it is held ED_EXPIRED.
- Given a practitioner type outside attorney/CPA/enrolled agent, When approval is requested, Then it is rejected.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Final §4966 regulations
- Revision of Rev. Proc. 2017-53

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-NPG-001#Q1**: Store provenance, scope, currency and reliance rights. Automatic reliance only on advice received from a qualified tax practitioner (directly or via a repository's practitioner-issued advice/reliance letter). Shared advice goes to review under the general good-faith standard (TD 9740 preamble).

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-NPG-001 decision and UNB-XIN-NPG-001 statement: Allows reliance when 'the practitioner or repository authorizes reliance'. → For the special rule the advice must be received from the qualified tax practitioner; repository-only authorization does not suffice (80 FR 57714).

### XID-NPG-002 — Confirm provisional decision: What purpose wording to an intermediary avoids an impermissible earmark for DAF grants?

**Disposition:** reasoned-interpretation · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** US federal · **Batch:** A (Charitable tax law)

**Original provisional answer.** Grants to US intermediaries may carry a non-binding preference ('to support [intermediary]'s work in [country/program]'). They may never name a specific foreign grantee as a required recipient or require pass-through. The intermediary must retain discretion and control. Wording is catalogued per intermediary.

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-XIN-NPG-002, requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv, requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Private-foundation analogue: a grant used by the grantee to pay a secondary grantee is not a grant to the secondary grantee if the grantor does not earmark it for a named secondary grantee and has no oral or written agreement to cause the selection; reason to believe is fine if the grantee controls selection independently. [EV-A-012, §53.4945-5(a)(6)(i)]
- *established* — Proposed anti-abuse rule: a series of distributions through intermediaries under a plan inconsistent with §4966 is treated as a single distribution. [EV-A-009, Preamble, Distributions (88 FR 77931)]
- *established* — Schedule F Part II includes grants to a domestic organization for the purpose of providing grants to a designated foreign organization. [EV-A-018, Part II scope]
- *interpretation* — Earmarked or conduit gifts are treated by reference to the ultimate recipient (IRS info letter; Rev. Rul. 63-252 principle). [EV-A-023, info letter 2024-0007]
- *established* — Market practice: non-binding 'special purpose' designations conveyed to the recipient, which the sponsor may modify. [EV-A-025, Special purpose designation]

**Supporting interpretation.** Non-binding preferences for the intermediary's own program in a country or cause, with the intermediary retaining discretion and control, are consistent with the §53.4945-5(a)(6) analogue. Naming a specific foreign grantee (even as a preference) risks being an earmark for a named secondary grantee.

**Competing interpretation.** Some intermediaries (e.g., 'friends of' organizations) accept donor-advised recommendations of specific foreign grantees and run their own ED/ER; with genuine intermediary discretion this is defensible but depends on facts.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Catalogue wording and capture donor intent separately (internal evidence) from outbound wording.

**Business assumptions.**

- Intermediaries are §170(b)(1)(A) organizations with their own foreign grant programs

**Recommended answer.** Confirm the decision with one clarification: outbound wording may name the intermediary's program, country or cause; it must not name any specific foreign organization, even as a non-binding suggestion, and no side agreement may let the donor or sponsor cause selection. Donor's original foreign recommendation is retained only as internal evidence. Report as a domestic grant; the intermediary handles Schedule F for its grants.

**Remaining uncertainty.** No final §4966 earmark rule; PF analogue applied by interpretation.

**Consequences if wrong.** Grant treated as made to the foreign organization: taxable distribution unless ED or ER (20%/5%).

**Conservative fallback.** Unrestricted grants to intermediaries only.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Offer intermediary 'country funds' as catalogue items (pooled programs) so donors choose a program, not a grantee: low friction, low earmark risk.

**External authorization required.** none

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given outbound purpose text containing the name of a foreign organization, When remittance is generated, Then it is blocked EARMARK_NAMED_SECONDARY_GRANTEE.
- Given an accepted intermediary alternative, When remitted, Then purpose text equals the catalogue's approved wording and the donor's foreign recommendation is stored internally only.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Final §4966 regulations with an intermediary rule
- IRS guidance on DAF grants via intermediaries

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-NPG-002#Q1**: A suggestion of a specific foreign grantee is risky even when non-binding (PF analogue §53.4945-5(a)(6)(i) refers to earmarking for 'any named secondary grantee'). Capture discretion and suggestion separately; never transmit a named foreign grantee; route ambiguous restrictions to review.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- none

### XID-NPG-004 — What criteria and maximum review period govern discretionary holds?

**Disposition:** business-policy-decision · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** Sponsor governance; US federal background · **Batch:** A (Charitable tax law)

**Original provisional answer.** Recommendation: Adjudicated findings or regulator actions only; 90-day maximum review.

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-XIN-NPG-006, UNB-EXT-005, requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/15-OPEN-DECISIONS-AND-SCENARIOS.md

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Market leader treats an organization as not an Eligible Grant Recipient where 'Fidelity Charitable has been informed that the organization is currently being investigated by governmental authorities regarding potential breaches of applicable law'; trustees may decline any grant. [EV-A-025, Eligible Grant Recipients, Compliance with law; closing grant paragraph]
- *established* — Sponsor has exclusive legal control over DAF assets; donors hold advisory privileges only. [EV-A-006, §170(f)(18); EV-A-001 §4966(d)(2)]
- *interpretation* — No statute or regulation requires a sponsor to make, or forbids it from holding, a grant to an eligible recipient; distributions to §170(b)(1)(A) organizations are excepted from §4966 tax regardless of allegations. [EV-A-001, §4966(c)(2)]
- *interpretation* — No federal source sets a maximum review period for discretionary holds. [EV-A-025, n/a]

**Supporting interpretation.** No law fixes criteria or duration; board policy controls.

**Competing interpretation.** Narrow criteria reduce perceived arbitrariness; broader criteria match market practice.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Platform enforces whatever criteria and maxima the board approves.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsor board approves policy annually

**Recommended answer.** Board-approved criteria: adjudicated findings, regulator actions, and official pending matters (indictment, AG or IRS action, government investigation the sponsor is informed of); media allegations only with elevated approval. Maximum review period 90 days, renewable once per new evidence, with automatic release. These are policy choices, not legal requirements.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Board preference.

**Consequences if wrong.** Donor relations; no tax consequence.

**Conservative fallback.** Adjudicated/regulator-only with 90 days.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Redirect offer instead of hold.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor board resolution approving DiscretionaryGrantPolicy.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a hold with evidence class not listed in the approved policy, When submitted, Then it is rejected.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Board policy review

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-NPG-006#Q1**: Indictment-triggered holds may be permitted under board policy.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- none

### XID-NPG-005 — Confirm provisional decision: Payee substitution to a merger successor: donor reconfirmation required?

**Disposition:** business-policy-decision · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** US federal; state nonprofit merger law (not researched) · **Batch:** A (Charitable tax law)

**Original provisional answer.** When a grantee merges, a legal successor with the same charitable purpose and an eligible status may receive open and recurring grants with sponsor approval and notice to the donor (no reconfirmation). If the purpose or recipient class changes, or the successor is not eligible, the grant is cancelled and the donor asked to re-recommend.

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-XIN-NPG-005, requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv, requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — The sponsor owns and controls DAF assets; donors hold only advisory privileges; contribution deduction depends on the sponsor's exclusive legal control. [EV-A-001, §4966(d)(2)(A)(ii)-(iii); EV-A-006 §170(f)(18)]
- *established* — TEOS/BMF reliance applies only to an organization listed under its official or recognized name and not before it is listed. [EV-A-016, Rev. Proc. 2018-32 §6.01-6.02]
- *interpretation* — Proposed rule analogue: a single-identified-organization fund may substitute another organization upon reorganization that ends the named organization, if beyond donor control. [EV-A-009, Prop. §53.4966-4(a)(5)]
- *established* — Market: donors have no legally enforceable right to any recommended grant; sponsor may decline. [EV-A-025, Grant section closing paragraph]

**Supporting interpretation.** No federal rule requires donor reconfirmation; the sponsor makes the grant. The legal requirement is that the successor itself be an eligible recipient verified on its own EIN.

**Competing interpretation.** Donor-relations and state AG donor-intent norms favor reconfirmation where the successor's program differs; some sponsors reconfirm all substitutions.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Implement as sponsor policy with independent eligibility evaluation of the successor.

**Business assumptions.**

- Successor's charitable purpose can be compared from filings

**Recommended answer.** Confirm as a sponsor policy: substitution to a verified legal successor that independently passes eligibility (own EIN, TEOS listing, same recipient class) with sponsor approval and donor notice; recurring grants should carry a notice-and-opt-out window before the next disbursement. Reconfirm when purpose, class or eligibility changes.

**Remaining uncertainty.** State merger-law treatment of restricted gifts to constituent corporations not verified.

**Consequences if wrong.** Donor complaints; possible grant to an ineligible successor if verification is skipped.

**Conservative fallback.** Always reconfirm.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Cancel and auto-propose a re-recommendation pre-filled with the successor: one-click donor action, no unilateral substitution.

**External authorization required.** none

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a MergerIntoSuccessor event, When open grants exist, Then grants are held until the successor passes eligibility on its own EIN and the sponsor approves.
- Given approval, When a recurring grant next disburses, Then the donor has received notice at least N days earlier (sponsor-configured) with an opt-out.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Donor complaint trend
- State AG guidance

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-NPG-005#Q1**: Permitted without reconfirmation under federal law; verify legal succession and the successor's own eligibility; sponsor policy decides notice versus reconfirmation.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- none

### XID-NPG-007 — Confirm provisional decision: Is a §4966 taxable distribution correctable, and what is the incident reporting channel (Form 4720) for sponsor and managers?

**Disposition:** established-by-authoritative-evidence · **Confidence:** high · **Jurisdiction:** US federal · **Batch:** A (Charitable tax law)

**Original provisional answer.** §4966 and §4967 first-tier taxes are 'qualified first tier taxes' under §4962 (subchapter G), so they are abatable if the event was due to reasonable cause, not wilful neglect, and is corrected within the correction period. The incident workflow therefore records reasonable-cause evidence and correction steps for every incident. Excise taxes are reported on Form 4720 by each liable person (sponsor; fund manager; donor/advisor for §4967). §4958 excess benefit is corrected within the correction period and also reported on Schedule L.

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-XIN-NPG-008, requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv, requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Qualified first tier taxes include any first tier tax imposed by subchapter G (added 2007); subchapter G is §§4966-4967. [EV-A-003, §4962(b); 2007 amendment note; EV-A-007]
- *established* — §§4966 and 4967 are first tier taxes and taxable events. [EV-A-004, §4963(a), (c)]
- *established* — Abatement requires the Secretary to be satisfied of reasonable cause, no willful neglect, and correction within the correction period. [EV-A-003, §4962(a)]
- *established* — No second tier tax exists for §§4966/4967, 'correct' is defined by the section imposing the second tier tax, and the correction period ends by reference to a second-tier deficiency notice, extended by periods the Secretary finds reasonable. [EV-A-004, §4963(b), (d)(1), (e)(1)]
- *established* — IRS: use Form 843 to request abatement under §4962 of first tier taxes under §§4942-4945, 4955, 4958, 4966 and 4967. [EV-A-017, Instructions for Form 4720 (2025), 'Abatement']
- *established* — Sponsor reports §4966 on Form 4720 Schedule K; fund managers and donors/advisors/related persons liable under §4967 file their own Form 4720 (Schedule L); §4958 is Schedule I; due 15th day of 5th month. [EV-A-017, Who Must File; Schedules I, K, L]
- *established* — Correction of a §4958(c)(2) DAF excess benefit may not be repaid into any DAF. [EV-A-005, §4958(f)(6)]

**Supporting interpretation.** Abatement is statutorily available for §4966 and §4967 first tier taxes, but 'correction' is undefined for them; in practice correction would mean recovering the amount and restoring it to charitable use, with the IRS setting the period. Abatement is discretionary and requested on Form 843.

**Competing interpretation.** Because there is no second tier tax or statutory correction definition, the IRS could find no correction mechanism and deny abatement except on its own terms.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Incident workflow records reasonable-cause evidence, recovery steps, and Form 4720/843 filing inputs for each liable person; it must not show liability as extinguished.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsor CPA prepares Form 4720

**Recommended answer.** The original answer is legally supported (abatement is available under §4962 for §4966/§4967 first tier taxes), and the review answer 'do not assert abatement' is too cautious. State it as: abatement may be requested on Form 843; it is discretionary and requires reasonable cause, no willful neglect, and correction, which is undefined for these taxes. Record recovery (repayment to the sponsor, not into any DAF for §4958(c)(2) cases), reasonable-cause evidence, and filing inputs: sponsor Form 4720 Schedule K; fund managers Schedule K Part II/L Part III; donors/advisors Schedule L; §4958 on Form 4720 Schedule I and Form 990 Schedule L Part I.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Meaning of 'correct' and the correction period end for §§4966/4967; IRS discretion.

**Consequences if wrong.** Liability not abated; penalties for non-filing if Form 4720 is missed.

**Conservative fallback.** Treat taxes as owed; pursue abatement as a claim.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Pre-disbursement controls (eligibility gates) reduce incidence; incident workflow remains for residual cases.

**External authorization required.** none

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given an ExciseTaxIncident for §4966, When opened, Then filing inputs list Form 4720 Schedule K for the sponsor and each approving fund manager, and a Form 843 abatement task with reasonable-cause and correction evidence fields.
- Given a §4958(c)(2) incident, When repayment is recorded, Then the repayment destination cannot be any DAF.
- Given an incident, When displayed, Then status never shows 'abated' unless an IRS determination is recorded.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Final §4966 regulations defining correction
- IRS guidance on §4962 for DAF taxes

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-NPG-008#Q1**: Yes, §4962 abatement extends to §4966/§4967 first tier taxes (subchapter G), requested on Form 843; unlike §4958 there is no statutory correction definition, so record recovery and reasonable-cause evidence and do not show relief until the IRS grants it.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-NPG-007 confirmation_review_answer: 'Do not assert section 4962 abatement' contradicts §4962(b) and the Form 4720 instructions. → Abatement is available but discretionary; correction undefined.
- XID-NPG-007 decision / UNB-XIN-NPG-008: '§4958 ... also reported on Schedule L' is ambiguous: Form 4720 Schedule L is §4967. → §4958 tax is on Form 4720 Schedule I; the transaction is disclosed on Form 990 Schedule L Part I.
- UNB-XIN-NPG-008 evidence: Cites 'unverified — general industry knowledge'. → Replace with 26 U.S.C. §§4962(b), 4963 and Form 4720 instructions (EV-A-003, -004, -017).

### RCD-017 — Confirm provisional decision: Funding model: currency reservation with haircut, cash sleeve, or pool buffer; and the percentage-grant fixing point.

**Disposition:** business-policy-decision · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** Reservation against capacity computed in the same transaction. Grant states Reserved → FundingPlanned → Funded before payment. Each pool keeps a cash liquidity buffer (sponsor-set percentage, default 2% of pool value) so routine grants fund from cash without per-grant trades. Percentage grants fix their amount at approval.

**Relevant requirements.** RCF-GRT-015, RCF-FIN-022, RCC-GRT-007, RCC-FIN-008, OD-13, REQ-X-002, UNB-RCN-FIN-008, UNB-RCN-FIN-009, unbound:requirements/reconciliation/2026-11-23/data/decisions.csv, unbound:requirements/grants/MULTI-INVESTMENT-FUNDING-AND-PERCENTAGE-GRANTS.md, unbound:requirements/grants/END-TO-END-GRANT-LIFECYCLE-AND-OPERATING-MODEL.md, UNB-XIN-ECO-015 (unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-24-question-resolutions-accounting-banking-economics.md)

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — GAAP requires NFPs to disclose qualitative and quantitative information on financial assets available for general expenditure within one year, considering donor, legal, contractual and internal board limits; it prescribes no liquidity buffer level. [EV-B-015, 958-210-50-1A(a)-(b)]
- *established* — Fidelity Charitable discloses $77.3B of financial assets available within one year (of ~$85.9B net assets) and treats Level 3, NAV-measured and PIF assets as unavailable; it discloses no fixed cash-buffer percentage. [EV-B-008, Note 2 Liquidity]
- *interpretation* — No statute, regulation or accounting standard found prescribes a per-pool cash buffer percentage (e.g. 2%) for DAF sponsors; the 2% default is a business assumption. [EV-B-015, 958-210-50-1A (disclosure only)]
- *interpretation* — Grant expense/payable is recognized at a measured amount when the unconditional promise is made (720-25-25-1); a percentage grant therefore needs a fixed amount at its recognition point. [EV-B-013, 720-25-25-1 (p.39)]

**Supporting interpretation.** Atomic capacity reservation plus explicit funding states prevents over-commitment; a pool-level cash sleeve lets routine grants settle without per-grant trades. Fixing a percentage grant's amount at approval aligns the reservation, the expense measurement and the donor-visible amount.

**Competing interpretation.** A fixed 2% buffer may be too small in December peaks or for illiquid pools and too large (cash drag) for steady pools; some sponsors liquidate per grant (Fidelity sells contributed property and pools carry cash per investment objective). A percentage could instead be fixed at payment (donor expectation of 'x% of balance at payment').

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound configures the buffer and state machine; the sponsor's investment committee owns the buffer level as investment/liquidity policy. Unbound must not ship 2% as an unlabeled default.

**Business assumptions.**

- Grant outflows are forecastable from history by pool and season
- Pools hold daily-liquid securities except designated illiquid sleeves
- Sponsor's investment policy statement permits a cash sleeve

**Recommended answer.** Keep atomic reservation in the same transaction and Reserved -> FundingPlanned -> Funded. Replace the fixed 2% default with a sponsor-set buffer per pool calibrated from a stress model (e.g. high-percentile net outflow over the settlement horizon plus December peak), with 2% allowed only as an explicitly labeled placeholder until 12 months of data exist. Fix percentage grants at approval (the amount that is reserved, communicated and expensed), with an option to re-fix only by a new approval.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Actual outflow distributions per sponsor/pool; whether any sponsor IPS forbids cash sleeves; donor expectation for percentage grants.

**Consequences if wrong.** Buffer too small: grant payment delays and forced intraday sales; too large: cash drag on donor accounts and investment-policy deviation.

**Conservative fallback.** Per-grant liquidation with T+settlement funding when the buffer is breached; block the grant at FundingPlanned until cash settles.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** (a) Fixed % buffer: low cost, simple, but miscalibrated (exposure: payment delays/cash drag). (b) Stress-calibrated buffer (recommended): modest analytics cost, low friction, low exposure. (c) Per-grant liquidation: no cash drag but trade cost, settlement delay and ops friction; loses same-day grant payment.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor investment committee (or board) approval of the liquidity/buffer policy within the investment policy statement.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given two concurrent grant approvals that together exceed fund capacity, when both commit, then exactly one succeeds and the other is rejected with no over-reservation.
- Given a pool with buffer policy version v, when buffer cash falls below the configured floor, then a replenishment trade instruction is generated and an alert raised, and the policy version is recorded on the event.
- Given a percentage grant of 10% approved when fund value is $50,000, when market value changes before payment, then the payable remains $5,000.
- Given no stress calibration exists for a pool, when the buffer default is displayed, then it is labeled 'placeholder - not calibrated'.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Two or more buffer breaches in a quarter
- Investment committee changes IPS
- Sponsor requests fix-at-payment percentage grants

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- RCD-017 decided_option: States a default 2% buffer without evidence; no authority or audited sponsor disclosure supports a universal 2%. → Make buffer sponsor-set and stress-calibrated; label any 2% value as an uncalibrated placeholder.

### RCD-018 — Confirm provisional decision: Fee base and posting: on what value, when, net of what, and payable to whom?

**Disposition:** business-policy-decision · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** Fee base is end-of-day fund market value, including reserved but unpaid amounts and excluding fees embedded in pool NAV. Fees accrue daily (actual/365) and settle monthly, with no fee on accrued fees. Minimum fees apply per sponsor schedule. Fees are recognized as sponsor revenue when settled.

**Relevant requirements.** RCF-FIN-012, RCF-FIN-013, UNB-RCN-FIN-005, UNB-ACC-010, UNB-XIN-ERP-002, unbound:requirements/FULL-ACCOUNTING-SYSTEM.md, unbound:requirements/accounting/CANONICAL-CHART-AND-POSTING-RULES.md §4 matrix row 'Sponsor fee assessed to a DAF', unbound:requirements/reconciliation/2026-11-23/data/decisions.csv

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Fidelity Charitable charges 0.60% of average Giving Account assets (or $100 minimum); for pools the fee is charged to the pool pro rata daily and affects NAV; for assets not in pools (including contributed property not yet sold) the fee is charged directly to the account. [EV-B-007, Fees and Expenses - General operating expenses]
- *established* — Vanguard Charitable's 0.60% fee accrues daily, reduces pool NAV and is presented as 'Net Assets Released for Administrative Fee' used to pay operating expenses - i.e. an internal release within the same entity, not third-party revenue. [EV-B-009, Note 7]
- *established* — The Boston Foundation funds operations by administrative fees 'allocated from other fund categories based upon those funds' market value' - an internal allocation. [EV-B-011, Note 1 Operating funds; (l) Administrative Fees]
- *established* — Foundation for the Carolinas reports 'Administrative fees received' of $7.0M as revenue on $4.4B of net assets and recognizes fees in the fiscal year in which they occur (accrual), not when settled. [EV-B-012, Statement of Activities; Note 2 Administrative Fees Received]
- *interpretation* — Internal fees charged by a sponsor to its own DAFs within the same legal entity are not revenue from an external party and are eliminated/presented as internal transfers on the entity's GAAP statements; fees from agency funds (liabilities) or other entities can be revenue. [EV-B-009, Note 7 (with EV-B-012 magnitude)]

**Supporting interpretation.** Daily accrual on end-of-day market value, monthly settlement and no fee-on-fee is consistent with Fidelity/Vanguard practice (daily NAV-based accrual). Excluding fees embedded in pool NAV avoids double charging.

**Competing interpretation.** Fee base could be average assets (Fidelity), quarterly balance (Boston DAFs), or 8-quarter average (Boston endowments). Including reserved-but-unpaid grants in the base is a pricing choice; including recognized grants payable is not, because those amounts are already expensed and no longer fund net assets.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound must support multiple versioned fee bases and day-count conventions and must post internal fees as interfund transfers with consolidated elimination, recognizing revenue only for fees from external payers (agency funds, partner invoices) on an accrual basis.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsor contracts define fee schedules
- Some sponsors host agency/pass-through funds whose fees are external revenue

**Recommended answer.** Version the fee basis per schedule (end-of-day market value default; average-balance and periodic-balance options), day count, accrual and settlement cadence, minimum, and payer source. Exclude grants already recognized as payable from the base; reserved-but-uncommunicated amounts are included by default (still fund net assets) and configurable. No fee on accrued fees; exclude fees embedded in pool NAV. Accounting: fees charged to the sponsor's own DAFs are internal transfers (fund-level expense / operating-fund recovery) eliminated in entity statements; fees from agency funds or other external payers are revenue recognized as the service period elapses (accrual), not on settlement.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Each sponsor auditor's presentation choice (gross internal fee lines vs net release); ASC 606 analysis for external fee revenue was not researched in depth.

**Consequences if wrong.** Recognizing internal fees as revenue overstates revenue and expense; cash-basis recognition misstates period results.

**Conservative fallback.** Post internal fees only as fund-dimension transfers with a zero entity-level P&L effect; treat external fee revenue on accrual with CPA review.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** (a) Single hard-coded base: cheapest, but fails sponsor contracts. (b) Versioned FeeBase contract (recommended): moderate build, low exposure. (c) Delegate fee calc to custodian NAV only: no platform calc, but loses per-account minimums/tiers and fund-level transparency.

**External authorization required.** Executed sponsor fee schedule / program terms; sponsor controller-approved fee accounting policy.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a DAF with fees charged by its own sponsor, when entity-level statements are generated, then fee entries net to zero across funds (eliminated) and no revenue line is produced.
- Given an agency fund charged a fee for March, when March closes unpaid, then fee revenue and a receivable are recognized in March.
- Given a grant recognized as payable, when the daily fee base is computed, then the payable amount is excluded.
- Given a fee schedule change effective on date D, when fees are computed across D, then each day uses the version in force that day.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Sponsor auditor requires different presentation
- New fee type with external payer

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- RCD-018 decided_option: 'Fees are recognized as sponsor revenue when settled' conflicts with GAAP accrual and with audited practice: internal DAF fees are internal releases/transfers (Vanguard Note 7; Boston allocations), and FFTC recognizes fees in the year they occur. → Internal fees: interfund transfer, eliminated; external fees: revenue on accrual over the service period.
- RCD-018 fee base: Including amounts already recognized as grants payable would charge fees on assets no longer in fund net assets. → Distinguish reserved (included, configurable) from recognized payable (excluded).

### RCD-019 — Confirm provisional decision: Contribution valuation, completed-gift dates, acknowledgment timing and returned contributions.

**Disposition:** reasoned-interpretation · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** Contributions credit grantable capacity only on Final: ACH after the sponsor-set return window (default 5 business days); wire on receipt; check on clear; securities on settlement into custody. Acknowledgment is issued on CompletedGiftDetermined ∧ SponsorAccepted. Accepted assets never return to a donor; returned grant funds go back to the originating fund.

**Relevant requirements.** RCF-FIN-001, RCF-FIN-002, RCF-FIN-004, RCF-FIN-007, RCF-FIN-020, RCF-FIN-021, RCF-REG-004, RR-UNB-ACC-006-r1, UNB-RCN-FIN-001, UNB-RCN-FIN-012, UNB-RCN-FIN-013, unbound:requirements/FULL-ACCOUNTING-SYSTEM.md, unbound:requirements/accounting/CANONICAL-CHART-AND-POSTING-RULES.md, unbound:requirements/reconciliation/2026-11-23/data/decisions.csv

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — A contribution is ordinarily made when delivery is effected; a mailed check that clears in due course is made on the mailing date. [EV-B-001, §1.170A-1(b)]
- *established* — An endorsed stock certificate is completed on delivery/mailing to the donee; if delivered to the donor's bank/broker or issuer for transfer, the gift is complete when transferred on the corporation's books. [EV-B-001, §1.170A-1(b)]
- *established* — Credit card gifts are deductible in the year charged; pay-by-phone gifts are delivered on the date the financial institution pays. [EV-B-002, 'When To Deduct']
- *established* — DAF contributions are deductible only with a CWA from the sponsor stating it has exclusive legal control; CWAs for >=$250 must state cash amount and describe (not value) noncash property, and be obtained by the earlier of the filing date or due date. [EV-B-003, §170(f)(8)(B)-(C); §170(f)(18)(B)]
- *established* — Donor tax FMV of traded securities is the mean of high and low selling prices on the valuation date - distinct from GAAP fair value used by the sponsor. [EV-B-004, 'Stocks and Bonds']
- *established* — Fidelity treats EFT contributions as timely if initiated by Dec 31 and wires/securities if received by Dec 31 (guidelines). [EV-B-006, deadline table]
- *established* — Fidelity records contributions on the date received and accepted; property not accepted is returned; accepted contributions are irrevocable and non-refundable. [EV-B-008, Note 2 Donor Contributions; EV-B-007 Contributions - General]
- *established* — ACH consumer debit return windows extend to 60 days for some return reasons (R11; R10 by rule), so a 5-business-day hold is a risk-based availability rule, not finality. [EV-B-005, R11 return timeframe]

**Supporting interpretation.** Gift completion (tax date), GAAP recognition (received/accepted), availability for granting (risk of reversal) and acknowledgment are four separate facts. For donor-initiated ACH (credit from donor bank or sponsor-pulled debit) no regulation fixes the date; by analogy to pay-by-phone, the gift is made when the donor's bank pays (settlement), with initiation date shown as a sponsor guideline (Fidelity). Card: date charged.

**Competing interpretation.** Some practitioners treat ACH debit gifts as made on the authorization/initiation date (Fidelity's year-end guideline) - a donor-favorable reading not confirmed by regulation. Electronic DTC securities transfers: date received in the sponsor's account (books-transfer analog) vs date donor instructed.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound records the facts and dates and applies sponsor rule tables; the sponsor issues the CWA and owns the completed-gift rule. CWA must not state a value for noncash property (only description), though sponsors may show a non-tax informational value clearly labeled.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsor accepts ACH, wire, check, card, DTC securities
- Sponsor reserves acceptance discretion (as Fidelity)

**Recommended answer.** Store separately: GiftCompletionDate (tax: check=mailing/postmark date if cleared; certificate=delivery/mailing; broker/DTC=date credited to sponsor account; card=charge date; ACH=settlement date with initiation date retained; wire=receipt), AcceptanceDate (GAAP recognition), AvailabilityDate (sponsor risk rule: e.g. ACH default 5 business days configurable, card per dispute risk; explicitly not finality), and AcknowledgmentDate (issued after acceptance; noncash described without value). If an ACH/card payment is later returned or charged back, reverse the contribution (it was never completed) and void/amend the acknowledgment; this is not a 'refund' of an accepted asset. Accepted assets are never refunded to donors; returned grant funds go back to the originating fund.

**Remaining uncertainty.** No IRS guidance explicitly fixes the gift date for ACH debits or DTC transfers; card-network dispute windows not verified here.

**Consequences if wrong.** Wrong tax year on acknowledgments (donor deduction disallowed or amended), grants funded from reversed money.

**Conservative fallback.** Acknowledge using settlement/receipt date; keep capacity unavailable until the sponsor's risk window passes; flag Dec 31/Jan 1 boundary items for manual review.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** (a) Single 'Final' date (original): simple but conflates tax date with availability (high exposure on year-end). (b) Four-date model (recommended): small schema cost, low exposure. (c) Accept only wires/DTC for large gifts: removes reversal risk but adds donor friction and loses card/ACH gifts.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor counsel/CPA sign-off of the completed-gift rule table and acknowledgment template (sponsor is the legal issuer).

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a check postmarked Dec 31 and deposited Jan 3 that clears, when the acknowledgment is generated, then the gift date is Dec 31.
- Given DTC shares credited to the sponsor account on Jan 2 after a Dec 30 instruction, when acknowledged, then the gift date is Jan 2 and the instruction date is retained.
- Given an ACH gift returned R10 on day 40, when the return posts, then the contribution and capacity are reversed and the acknowledgment is voided or amended.
- Given a noncash gift >= $250, when the CWA is generated, then it describes the property without stating a tax value and states exclusive legal control.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- IRS guidance on electronic contribution dates
- Finalized §4966/§170(f)(18) regulations
- Sponsor counsel opinion

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- RCD-019 decided_option: 'ACH final after 5 business days' treats a risk hold as finality; ACH consumer returns can arrive up to 60 days. → Name it AvailabilityDate (risk rule), separate from GiftCompletionDate and Final.
- RCD-019 'Accepted assets never return to a donor': A returned/charged-back payment is not an accepted asset being refunded; the contribution never completed. → Model reversal of incomplete contributions distinctly from (prohibited) refunds.

### RCD-020 — Confirm provisional decision: Reconciliation: break-blocking thresholds and owners, attestation versus auto-pass, omnibus allocation method.

**Disposition:** business-policy-decision · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** Continuous automated reconciliation. Routine matched items auto-pass. Independent sampled attestation of at least 5% of passes per month. Any unexplained cash break blocks statement publication for the affected funds. Security price breaks follow the sponsor tolerance. Allocation method is declared per custody account. Fund and restriction are balancing segments.

**Relevant requirements.** RCF-FIN-016, RCF-FIN-018, RCF-FIN-019, RCF-FIN-010, RCF-FIN-034, UNB-RCN-FIN-006, UNB-RCN-FIN-007, UNB-XIN-ADV-002, UNB-XIN-ECO-004, unbound:requirements/FULL-ACCOUNTING-SYSTEM.md, unbound:requirements/reconciliation/2026-11-23/data/decisions.csv, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-24-open-decision-resolution-review.md

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Exclusive reliance on any percentage or numerical materiality threshold has no basis in the accounting literature or the law; qualitative factors matter. [EV-B-022, SAB 99 Topic 1.M.1]
- *established* — Audit planning materiality for NFPs is a judgment; ISA 320 gives 1% of total revenue or expenses as an example, with higher or lower percentages possible. [EV-B-023, ISA 320 para A7]
- *interpretation* — No authoritative source found prescribes a 5% attestation sample of automated reconciliations; sample sizes are a control-design choice. [EV-B-023, (absence finding)]

**Supporting interpretation.** Continuous automated matching with auto-pass for exact matches and independent review on a risk-based sample is consistent with modern control design; blocking statement publication for affected funds on unexplained cash breaks protects donors.

**Competing interpretation.** Some controllers will require full monthly sign-off of bank/custody reconciliations by a named preparer and reviewer regardless of automation; auditors may test the automated control's design and operation instead of samples.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound provides tolerances, sampling engine and blocking; sponsor controller sets thresholds and sample rates. Materiality for statement-blocking must be per sponsor, with absolute and relative components, plus zero tolerance for unexplained cash breaks on affected funds.

**Business assumptions.**

- Daily bank/custody feeds available
- Controller exists at each sponsor

**Recommended answer.** Continuous reconciliation; exact matches auto-pass; independent attestation of a risk-stratified sample whose size is set by the controller (statistically or judgmentally, with increased sampling after errors) - 5% is not a requirement and must be labeled a configurable starting value. Unexplained cash breaks block statements only for affected funds; security price/position tolerances absolute+relative per asset class. Monthly controller sign-off on reconciliation completeness. Allocation method declared per custody account; fund and restriction as balancing segments.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Sponsor auditor expectations for reliance on automated controls (SOC 1 of Unbound may be needed).

**Consequences if wrong.** Too lax: undetected misstatement; too strict: blocked statements and ops cost.

**Conservative fallback.** Monthly preparer/reviewer sign-off of every account reconciliation in addition to automation.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** (a) Attest every reconciliation: high labor, low exposure. (b) Risk-based sampling + automated control evidence + SOC 1 (recommended): moderate cost, lets auditors rely on controls. (c) Auto-pass only: lowest cost, high exposure.

**External authorization required.** none

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given an unexplained cash break of any amount in fund F, when statements are generated, then F's statement is blocked and others publish.
- Given controller sets sample rate r and stratum weights, when month closes, then the sample drawn is reproducible from a seed and attested items recorded with reviewer identity independent from the preparer.
- Given an error found in the sample, when next month's sample is drawn, then the rate for that stratum increases per policy.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Audit finding on reconciliation controls
- Repeated breaks in a stratum

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- RCD-020 decided_option: 'at least 5% of passes per month' presented as a decided minimum with no authority. → Make sample rate controller-set and risk-based; label 5% as configurable starting value.

### RCD-035 — Confirm provisional decision: Must every NAV have a human checker?

**Disposition:** business-policy-decision · **Confidence:** high · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** In-tolerance NAVs are published by deterministic policy (A3) under sponsor delegation, with a sampled human review the next business day. Out-of-tolerance NAVs need a human approver (A4).

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-NAV-008, RCF-WF-001, unbound:requirements/FULL-ACCOUNTING-SYSTEM.md, unbound:requirements/reconciliation/2026-11-23/data/decisions.csv, unbound:requirements/ENTERPRISE-GAP-REGISTER-AND-CROSS-DOMAIN-SCENARIOS.md

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Charitable organizations and pooled funds they maintain for charitable assets are excluded from the Investment Company Act definition of investment company, so registered-fund NAV rules do not govern DAF pools. [EV-B-024, 15 USC 80a-3(c)(10)]
- *established* — For registered funds, Rule 2a-5 requires a board or valuation designee to assess valuation risk, apply and test methodologies and oversee pricing services - it does not require a human checker per NAV; it is a useful analog only. [EV-B-025, Rule summary]
- *established* — Fidelity Charitable values investments using independent pricing services approved by the Trustees. [EV-B-008, Note 2 Investments]

**Supporting interpretation.** Deterministic publication of in-tolerance NAVs under a documented delegation, with exceptions (stale/missing prices, tolerance breaches, corporate actions) to a human approver and a risk-based after-the-fact sample, matches the Rule 2a-5 oversight model.

**Competing interpretation.** Some sponsors' investment committees may require daily human sign-off; auditors may prefer it when pricing feeds are from a single vendor.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Applies to Unbound pool NAV publishing; delegation must be recorded per sponsor.

**Business assumptions.**

- Independent pricing vendors
- Tolerance rules defined per asset class

**Recommended answer.** No law or standard requires a human checker for every NAV. Publish in-tolerance NAVs by deterministic policy under a recorded investment-committee delegation; route out-of-tolerance, stale, missing or manually overridden prices to a human approver before publication; perform a risk-based next-business-day independent sample; block affected publication on unresolved breaks; review the delegation and tolerances at least annually.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Individual sponsor IPS/committee charters.

**Consequences if wrong.** Mispriced NAV affects fees, grants and donor statements; correction requires reprocessing.

**Conservative fallback.** Human approval of every NAV until the delegation is executed.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** (a) Human checks all NAVs: daily labor, low exposure. (b) Delegated deterministic publication + exceptions (recommended). (c) Custodian-published NAV only: removes Unbound valuation exposure but depends on custodian SLAs.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor investment committee/board resolution delegating NAV publication and approving tolerances and pricing sources.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given no recorded delegation for sponsor S, when a NAV is computed, then it requires human approval.
- Given a price older than the staleness limit, when NAV computes, then publication is blocked and an exception created.
- Given an in-tolerance NAV, when published automatically, then the policy version and inputs are stored and eligible for sampling.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Valuation error found
- Change in pricing vendor
- Committee policy change

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- none

### RCD-036 — Confirm provisional decision: Minimum fee versus reserved grant capacity (OD-10)

**Disposition:** business-policy-decision · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** Reserved grants always take priority. A fee shortfall is carried as a receivable against future capacity, waivable by A4. It never draws reserved capacity.

**Relevant requirements.** RCF-WF-001, OD-10, unbound:requirements/FULL-ACCOUNTING-SYSTEM.md, unbound:requirements/reconciliation/2026-11-23/14-OPEN-DECISIONS.md, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-24-confirmation-question-review.md

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Fidelity charges a $100 minimum annual fee; where 0.60% is below $100 the difference is assessed at fiscal year end, prorated for accounts funded during the year. [EV-B-007, Fees and Expenses - General operating expenses]
- *established* — Donors have no legal or beneficial interest in DAF sub-accounts; all assets are the sponsor's. [EV-B-008, Note 2 Net Assets Without Donor Restrictions]
- *interpretation* — A grant becomes a liability (payable) only when an unconditional promise is made; a reservation not yet communicated is an internal earmark, not a liability. [EV-B-013, 720-25-25-1]

**Supporting interpretation.** Because the sponsor owns all assets, priority between internal fees and reserved grants is sponsor policy. Communicated/recognized grants are liabilities and must rank ahead of internal fees; uncommunicated reservations can also be protected by policy to keep donor-facing commitments.

**Competing interpretation.** Fee-first ensures the operating fund is paid; a 'receivable' from a DAF with no balance is not an asset in entity GAAP statements (internal) and may never be collected.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound implements priority, shortfall tracking and waiver workflow; the sponsor sets policy.

**Business assumptions.**

- Minimum fees exist in sponsor schedules

**Recommended answer.** Recognized grants payable always rank ahead of fees; reserved (uncommunicated) grants also rank ahead by default policy. A fee shortfall is tracked as a fund-level memo/interfund claim against future capacity (not an entity-level asset), waivable by an authorized approver with reason; collect from the next available capacity; write off on fund closure.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Sponsor program terms may give fees priority; auditor view on interfund receivable presentation.

**Consequences if wrong.** Grant failure for donor-communicated commitments or uncollected fees.

**Conservative fallback.** Block new reservations when projected fees exceed residual capacity.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** (a) Fee-first: protects operating revenue, risks grant failure. (b) Grants-first with memo claim (recommended). (c) Assess minimum fee only at year end (Fidelity model): simplest, shortfall rarely arises intra-year.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor program terms/fee schedule stating priority and waiver authority.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a fund with $50 free capacity, $200 reserved and $100 minimum fee due, when fees assess, then $50 is charged, $50 recorded as fee claim, and the reservation is untouched.
- Given a waiver by an authorized approver, when recorded, then the claim is cleared with reason and approver identity.
- Given entity-level statements, when generated, then fee claims are eliminated and not presented as receivables from third parties.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Sponsor program terms change
- Audit finding

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- RCD-036 'carried as a receivable': An intra-entity fee shortfall is not a receivable on the sponsor's GAAP statements. → Call it a fund-level fee claim (memo/interfund), eliminated at entity level.

### XID-ADV-005 — Confirm provisional decision: Routing of prior-year adjustments discovered during and after the audit (AuditAdjustment period vs PriorPeriodAdjustment).

**Disposition:** reasoned-interpretation · **Confidence:** high · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** Before the audited statements are issued, adjustments found during the audit post in a soft-closed AuditAdjustment period (period 13) and accept only journals linked to an AuditFinding. After issuance, the XID-EVA-002 route applies.

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-XIN-ACC-005, UNB-XIN-EVA-002, unbound:requirements/FULL-ACCOUNTING-SYSTEM.md, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Entities that are not public entities evaluate subsequent events through the date statements are 'available to be issued' (complete and all approvals obtained); public entities, which include conduit bond obligors, use the issued date. [EV-B-021, FAS 165 paras 5-7 (codified ASC 855-10)]
- *established* — Recognized subsequent events providing evidence about balance-sheet-date conditions adjust the statements. [EV-B-021, FAS 165 para 8]
- *established* — Errors found after issuance require restatement (if material). [EV-B-020, FAS 154 para 25]
- *established* — Fidelity Charitable evaluated subsequent events through the available-to-be-issued date (Oct 29, 2025). [EV-B-008, Note 2 Basis of Accounting]

**Supporting interpretation.** Before the statements are available to be issued, audit adjustments are simply corrections of draft statements; a controlled period-13 that accepts only finding-linked journals preserves the closed period 12 and gives an audit trail.

**Competing interpretation.** Some ERPs reopen period 12; others post audit adjustments as top-side entries outside the ledger.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Boundary event must be configurable per sponsor (available-to-be-issued vs issued) because some sponsors are conduit bond obligors.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsor audit cycle with management representation date

**Recommended answer.** Adopt period 13 (AuditAdjustment) accepting only AuditFinding-linked journals until the statements are available to be issued (non-public NFPs) or issued (public entities incl. conduit bond obligors); thereafter route per XID-EVA-002. Record the boundary date and basis per fiscal year.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Auditor preference for top-side vs ledger entries.

**Consequences if wrong.** Adjustments posted into wrong period or after issuance without restatement analysis.

**Conservative fallback.** Lock period 13 at report date and require CFO approval for any later entry.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Reopen period 12 (simpler, loses immutability) vs period 13 (recommended) vs top-side only (loses ledger traceability).

**External authorization required.** none

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given period 13 open, when a journal without an AuditFinding link is submitted, then it is rejected.
- Given sponsor flagged conduit bond obligor, when boundary is computed, then the issued date is used; otherwise available-to-be-issued date.
- Given boundary passed, when an adjustment is submitted, then it routes to the XID-EVA-002 flow.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Sponsor becomes conduit obligor
- Auditor requirement

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-ADV-005 decision_outcome 'boundary set at issuance date': For non-public NFPs ASC 855 uses the available-to-be-issued date (Fidelity practice); 'issued' applies to public entities including conduit bond obligors. → Make boundary per sponsor: available-to-be-issued (default) or issued (public/conduit).

### XID-ADV-006 — Confirm provisional decision: Which GrantRecognitionPoint values are permitted?

**Disposition:** reasoned-interpretation · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** Permitted GrantRecognitionPoint values: ApprovalCommunicatedToGrantee (default) and PaymentReleased (only where payment is the first communication to the grantee). SponsorApproval is NOT a selectable policy; it collapses into ApprovalCommunicatedToGrantee when the approval notice is sent in the same period. Conditional grants (barrier plus right of return/release) are recognized when the barrier is overcome.

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-XIN-ACC-002, UNB-ACC-003, UNB-ACC-033, ACC-MDL-002, unbound:requirements/FULL-ACCOUNTING-SYSTEM.md, unbound:requirements/accounting/CANONICAL-CHART-AND-POSTING-RULES.md, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Contributions made are expensed in the period made; unconditional promises to give cash are recognized as payables and contribution expense. [EV-B-013, 720-25-25-1 (ASU 2018-08 pp.39-40)]
- *established* — A donor-imposed condition requires a barrier and a right of return/release; ambiguous stipulations are presumed conditional; conditional promises are not recognized until the barrier is overcome. [EV-B-013, 958-605-25-5A to 25-5E]
- *established* — Pre-2018 Codification text (958-720-25-2, superseded by ASU 2018-08) said an unconditional promise is recognized when the donor has an obligation, which 'generally occurs when the donor approves a specific grant or when the recipient of the promise is notified'; donor-reserved rescission rights prevented recognition. [EV-B-013, 958-720-25-2 superseded text (p.36)]
- *established* — Audited sponsor practice recognizes unconditional grants when approved: Colorado Gives ('when they are approved'), Boston Foundation ('when approved by the Board or committed to grantees'), FFTC ('when approved and all conditions have been met'), Vanguard (expense when authorized). [EV-B-010, Note 2 Grants (also EV-B-011 (i), EV-B-012 Note 9, EV-B-009 Note 2)]
- *interpretation* — A promise to give is an agreement; recognition requires verifiable documentation that the promise was made and received, implying communication to the recipient. [EV-B-028, article body]

**Supporting interpretation.** Requiring communication prevents expensing internal approvals that the sponsor can still freely cancel; Unbound sends the notice at approval, so approval and communication fall in the same period in normal operation.

**Competing interpretation.** The removed Codification text and current audited practice accept approval as the recognition event where the sponsor's approval creates an obligation; DAF sponsors approve grants for payment within days and rarely rescind. Removing SponsorApproval as a selectable policy contradicts majority audited practice.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound must not hard-code an accounting policy that differs from sponsors' audited policies; it must capture approval, communication, conditions and rescission rights independently and let the sponsor's policy choose the trigger.

**Business assumptions.**

- Unbound auto-sends approval notices
- Sponsors retain the right to cancel before payment in program terms

**Recommended answer.** Permitted values: SponsorApproval (allowed where the sponsor's CPA-documented policy treats approval as creating the obligation and the sponsor does not reserve a free rescission right), ApprovalCommunicatedToGrantee (platform default), PaymentReleased (only where payment is the first communication). Conditional grants recognized when the barrier is overcome. Always capture approval, communication, condition and rescission evidence; produce a period-end cutoff report of approved-but-uncommunicated items.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Current Codification wording of 958-605/958-405/958-720 on recognition timing not read (paywalled); auditor positions vary.

**Consequences if wrong.** Expense/payable cutoff misstatements at year end (usually small because approval-to-payment is days).

**Conservative fallback.** Default ApprovalCommunicatedToGrantee with same-moment notification, eliminating the timing difference.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** (a) Single fixed trigger: simple but conflicts with some audited policies. (b) Policy enumeration with evidence capture (recommended). (c) Make approval and notification atomic so the choice is immaterial: low cost, removes most exposure; loses only deferred-notice workflows.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor management's written grant-recognition accounting policy, accepted by the sponsor's external auditor in the first audited year.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given policy ApprovalCommunicatedToGrantee and a grant approved Dec 31 with notice sent Jan 2, when Dec closes, then no expense is recognized in Dec and the item appears on the cutoff report.
- Given policy SponsorApproval without CPA policy evidence attached, when a sponsor tries to activate it, then activation is blocked.
- Given a grant with a measurable barrier and right of release, when approved, then no expense is recognized until the barrier is recorded as met.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Sponsor auditor policy statement
- FASB amendment to 958-605/720-25

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-ADV-006 decision_outcome (SponsorApproval not selectable): Rests on 'promise requires communication' cited as secondary knowledge with the Codification unread; superseded 958-720-25-2 expressly allowed approval OR notification and four audited sponsors recognize at approval. → Restore SponsorApproval as a selectable, evidence-gated policy; keep communicated as default.
- XID-ADV-006 decision_record.rationale: Cites ASC 720-25-25-1 as establishing communication; the paragraph only says period made and payables for unconditional promises. → Cite 720-25-25-1 for measurement/period only; communication is interpretation.

### XID-ECO-005 — Confirm provisional decision: Relationship between management ABC and Form 990 functional expense allocation

**Disposition:** established-by-authoritative-evidence · **Confidence:** high · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** Management activity-based costing and the GAAP/Form 990 functional expense allocation use the same activity dataset with two separately governed mappings. The functional mapping is accounting policy, approved annually by the controller and external CPA, and is the only one that reaches financial statements. The management mapping never posts.

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-ACC-019, RR-UNB-ACC-014-r1, UNB-XIN-ECO-001, unbound:requirements/FULL-ACCOUNTING-SYSTEM.md, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — All NFPs must present expenses by both function and nature in one location, and disclose methods used to allocate costs among program and support functions. [EV-B-015, 958-720-45-15; 958-720-50-1(bb),(d)]
- *established* — Form 990 Part IX uses the organization's normal accounting method; if the accounting system does not allocate expenses, any reasonable allocation method may be used, reported accurately and documented. [EV-B-017, Part IX introduction]
- *established* — Return should generally use the same accounting method as the books. [EV-B-017, General Instructions - Accounting method]
- *interpretation* — No authority governs internal management activity-based costing; it is management information. [EV-B-015, (absence)]

**Supporting interpretation.** One activity dataset, two governed mappings: the functional mapping is accounting policy feeding GAAP and Part IX; the ABC mapping never posts.

**Competing interpretation.** Using one mapping for both reduces cost but lets management changes alter reported functional expenses.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Applies to sponsor operating expenses and Unbound's cost-attribution engine.

**Business assumptions.**

- Time/activity data is captured

**Recommended answer.** Adopt as decided, with one correction: the functional allocation method is management's accounting policy (controller/CFO approves annually and documents it); the external auditor audits it and the preparer uses it for Part IX - the CPA does not 'approve' it.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Joint-cost allocation (958-720-45-28 et seq.) not researched.

**Consequences if wrong.** Functional expense misstatement; Part IX/GAAP mismatch.

**Conservative fallback.** Single functional mapping only; ABC derived as non-posting view.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Independent datasets (higher cost, reconciliation burden) vs shared dataset (recommended).

**External authorization required.** none

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a management ABC mapping change, when statements regenerate, then functional expense totals are unchanged.
- Given the functional mapping version v approved by the controller, when Part IX is produced, then it uses v and the allocation-method disclosure text is generated.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Auditor finding
- Change in programs

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-ECO-005 confirmation_required 'External CPA approves the functional mapping annually': No requirement for CPA approval; allocation is management's policy, audited by the auditor. → Controller/CFO approves; auditor reviews in audit.

### XID-EVA-002 — Confirm provisional decision: Default treatment for postings whose effective date falls in a hard-closed period: current-period prior-period adjustment, reopen, or restatement, and the materiality th…

**Disposition:** reasoned-interpretation · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** A posting whose effective date falls in a hard-closed period posts in the current open period with relates_to_period and a correction class. If the affected statements have not been issued, see XID-ADV-005. If they have been issued: immaterial → current-period correction with disclosure ('little r' revision where comparatives shown); material → formal restatement of comparatives, with originally issued statements preserved. A hard-closed period is reopened only before issuance and only with controller + CFO approval. Materiality is set annually by the CFO with the auditor (illustrative quantitative default 0.5% of total net assets or 5% of the change in net assets, whichever is lower, plus qualitative factors). Donor-facing fund statements already issued are always corrected by an adjustment line with explanation on the next statement, and an amended statement is issued whenever an acknowledgment or a tax-relevant figure changes.

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-ACC-017, ACC-ADR-011, UNB-XIN-EVA-002, ACC-MDL-010, unbound:requirements/FULL-ACCOUNTING-SYSTEM.md, unbound:requirements/accounting/CANONICAL-CHART-AND-POSTING-RULES.md, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — An error in prior-period statements discovered after issuance is reported as a prior-period adjustment by restating prior statements (cumulative effect to opening net assets of the first period presented; period-specific effects corrected) with disclosure. [EV-B-020, FAS 154 paras 25-26 (codified ASC 250-10-45-23 and 250-10-50-7; paragraph mapping not verified)]
- *established* — Materiality cannot rest exclusively on a numerical threshold; qualitative factors apply. [EV-B-022, SAB 99 Topic 1.M.1]
- *established* — Auditor planning materiality example for NFPs is 1% of total revenue or expenses, adjustable by judgment. [EV-B-023, ISA 320 A7]
- *interpretation* — Concepts Statement 8 Ch.3 (as amended 2018) treats materiality as entity-specific; FASB sets no uniform threshold (secondary, not fetched). [EV-B-027, search snippet]
- *interpretation* — The 'little r' revision approach is SEC staff practice (SAB 108) and was not verified here for NFPs. [EV-B-022, n/a]

**Supporting interpretation.** Posting late items in the open period with relates_to_period preserves closed-period immutability; restatement only when material to issued statements follows ASC 250.

**Competing interpretation.** Some sponsors prefer reopening before issuance; an immaterial error that would be material if corrected in the current period requires revision of comparatives.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound implements routing, evidence and approvals; materiality is a sponsor/auditor judgment input, not a platform constant.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsor has CFO and auditor
- Donor statements issued periodically

**Recommended answer.** Keep: effective date and posting period stored separately; post in current open period with relates_to_period and correction class; before issuance see XID-ADV-005; after issuance, CFO determines materiality (with auditor input) - material: restate comparatives and preserve originally issued statements; immaterial: correct in current period, and revise comparatives if correcting currently would itself be material. Remove the '0.5% of net assets or 5% of change' default or label it 'illustrative, non-authoritative'; require a recorded qualitative assessment. Controller approval matrix risk-based (amount, cumulative, unusual, related-party). Donor statements: adjustment line on next statement; amended acknowledgment whenever an acknowledged amount or date changes.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Codification text not read; AU-C 320 text not fetched.

**Consequences if wrong.** Failure to restate when required or unnecessary restatements.

**Conservative fallback.** Escalate any correction to issued periods to CFO with auditor consultation before posting.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** (a) Always reopen: breaks immutability. (b) Current-period posting with relates_to_period (recommended). (c) Separate restatement ledger version: higher build cost, cleanest comparatives.

**External authorization required.** none

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a hard-closed issued period and a correction, when posted, then it lands in the open period with relates_to_period and correction class, and the original issued statement version is retained.
- Given a correction classified Material by the CFO, when statements regenerate, then restated comparatives and disclosure fields are produced.
- Given no recorded qualitative materiality assessment, when classifying, then classification cannot complete.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Auditor finding
- Restatement event

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-EVA-002#Q1**: No authoritative threshold; use a risk-based approval matrix (amount, cumulative, unusual, related-party) set by the CFO; numeric values are sponsor policy.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-EVA-002 decision_outcome illustrative default '0.5% of total net assets or 5% of the change in net assets': No authoritative basis; SAB 99 rejects exclusive numerical thresholds and auditor examples use other benchmarks (1% revenue/expenses for NFPs). → Delete or label as non-authoritative illustration; require qualitative assessment.

### XID-INV-001 — Confirm provisional decision: Which GrantRecognitionPoint does each sponsor adopt, and does approval without notice to the charity create a promise to give?

**Disposition:** reasoned-interpretation · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** Default recognition point for every sponsor is ApprovalCommunicatedToGrantee; the platform sends the approval notice automatically on approval, so in normal operation expense, payable and approval fall in the same period. Approval without notice does not create a promise to give.

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-XIN-ACC-002, OD-19, unbound:requirements/FULL-ACCOUNTING-SYSTEM.md, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-24-question-resolutions-accounting-banking-economics.md

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Contributions made are expensed in the period made; unconditional promises to give cash are recognized as payables and contribution expense. [EV-B-013, 720-25-25-1 (ASU 2018-08 pp.39-40)]
- *established* — A donor-imposed condition requires a barrier and a right of return/release; ambiguous stipulations are presumed conditional; conditional promises are not recognized until the barrier is overcome. [EV-B-013, 958-605-25-5A to 25-5E]
- *established* — Pre-2018 Codification text (958-720-25-2, superseded by ASU 2018-08) said an unconditional promise is recognized when the donor has an obligation, which 'generally occurs when the donor approves a specific grant or when the recipient of the promise is notified'; donor-reserved rescission rights prevented recognition. [EV-B-013, 958-720-25-2 superseded text (p.36)]
- *established* — Audited sponsor practice recognizes unconditional grants when approved: Colorado Gives ('when they are approved'), Boston Foundation ('when approved by the Board or committed to grantees'), FFTC ('when approved and all conditions have been met'), Vanguard (expense when authorized). [EV-B-010, Note 2 Grants (also EV-B-011 (i), EV-B-012 Note 9, EV-B-009 Note 2)]
- *interpretation* — A promise to give is an agreement; recognition requires verifiable documentation that the promise was made and received, implying communication to the recipient. [EV-B-028, article body]

**Supporting interpretation.** An undisclosed internal approval the sponsor can reverse is an intention, not a promise; communication creates the recipient's expectation and evidence of a promise 'received'.

**Competing interpretation.** Where the sponsor's governance makes approval binding and payment follows in days, approval itself evidences the obligation (historic 958-720-25-2 text; audited practice).

**Applicability to Unbound.** Platform default only; the sponsor's policy governs its statements.

**Business assumptions.**

- Notices are sent automatically at approval

**Recommended answer.** Platform default ApprovalCommunicatedToGrantee with notice sent atomically at approval, so approval, communication, expense and payable fall in the same period. Whether approval without notice creates a promise is not settled by publicly verifiable authority: treat it as the sponsor's documented accounting policy (SponsorApproval permitted with evidence), not a platform rule.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Codification text not read; auditor-specific views.

**Consequences if wrong.** Small cutoff differences at period end.

**Conservative fallback.** Atomic approve-and-notify.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Atomic approve-and-notify makes the legal/accounting question moot for nearly all grants at negligible cost; deferred-notice cases (e.g. anonymous or scheduled grants) route through cutoff review.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor accounting policy accepted by its external auditor.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given approval at time t, when the grant is approved, then a grantee notice is queued at t and its send timestamp recorded.
- Given a scheduled grant with notice deferred, when period ends between approval and notice, then it appears on the cutoff report.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Auditor disagreement
- Codification text reviewed

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-ACC-002#Q1**: Not settled by public authority; superseded 958-720-25-2 recognized at approval or notification and audited sponsors use approval. Record approval, communication, conditions and rescission rights separately; default to communicated-at-approval; allow SponsorApproval under documented policy.
- **XID-ADV-006**: Permit SponsorApproval (evidence-gated), ApprovalCommunicatedToGrantee (default), PaymentReleased (payment as first communication); conditional grants on barrier met.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-INV-001 decision_outcome: States as fact that approval without notice does not create a promise; authority cited as unread secondary knowledge. → Reclassify as interpretation/policy; cite superseded 958-720-25-2 and audited practice as counter-evidence.

### XID-INV-002 — Confirm provisional decision: Is the DAF component presented as net assets without donor restrictions for every program version, and which account types are agency/pass-through?

**Disposition:** established-by-authoritative-evidence · **Confidence:** high · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** Classification by account type: (1) donor-advised funds whose program documents grant the sponsor variance power: contribution revenue, net assets WITHOUT donor restrictions; (2) funds where a nonprofit transfers assets and names itself beneficiary (agency/designated funds) without granting variance power: LIABILITY, not revenue; (3) only gift instruments imposing a time or purpose restriction beyond advisory privileges create net assets WITH donor restrictions. Each program version must store the variance-power clause; missing evidence blocks classification (1).

**Relevant requirements.** RR-UNB-ACC-033-r1, UNB-XIN-ACC-001, UNB-ACC-033, ACC-MDL-008, unbound:requirements/FULL-ACCOUNTING-SYSTEM.md, unbound:requirements/accounting/CANONICAL-CHART-AND-POSTING-RULES.md, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-24-question-resolutions-accounting-banking-economics.md

**Authoritative findings.**
