- *established* — A recipient directed to distribute to a specified unaffiliated beneficiary is a donee (contribution) if the donor explicitly grants variance power - unilateral power to redirect, explicitly referred to in the transfer instrument. [EV-B-014, FAS 136 para 12 (codified in ASC 958-605; Codification paragraph number not verified)]
- *established* — A transfer is a liability of the recipient, not a contribution, if the provider retains a unilateral right to redirect, the transfer is revocable/repayable or accompanied by a conditional promise, the provider controls the recipient, or the provider names itself/its affiliate as beneficiary (unless an equity transaction between financially interrelated entities). [EV-B-014, FAS 136 paras 17(a)-(d), 18]
- *established* — Where a nonprofit transfers assets naming itself beneficiary, the community foundation recognizes a liability even though the nonprofit explicitly granted variance power. [EV-B-014, FAS 136 App. A paras 53-56]
- *established* — Colorado Gives records agency endowments and agency designations as liabilities ($69.7M at 12/31/2025). [EV-B-010, Note 2 Agency Endowments and Designations; Note 7]
- *established* — Vanguard classifies its DAFs as net assets without donor restrictions; Fidelity reports $85.8B without and $89M with donor restrictions, including 'accounts with donor-imposed limitations on use' and PIF remainder interests. [EV-B-009, Note 2 (and EV-B-008 Note 2 Net Assets With Donor Restrictions)]
- *established* — Boston Foundation classifies all funds except perpetual endowments as without donor restrictions because of its variance power - variance power does not remove perpetual time restrictions. [EV-B-011, Note 1 variance power paragraph]
- *established* — Community foundation National Standards require variance power in fund agreements; hybrid agency funds may split agency vs third-party gifts. [EV-B-026, FAQ: Agency Endowment Funds]

**Supporting interpretation.** DAF advisory privileges are not donor restrictions; with explicit variance power and sponsor exclusive legal control, DAF contributions are revenue without donor restrictions. Gift instruments imposing time (e.g. perpetual) or purpose restrictions create with-donor-restriction net assets.

**Competing interpretation.** Some auditors classify certain designated/field-of-interest funds as with donor restrictions despite variance power (Fidelity's with-restriction accounts); pass-through workplace programs where donors can redirect or obtain refunds fail the variance-power test and are liabilities (para 17(a)-(b)).

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound's AccountingTreatment enumeration must be driven by the transfer instrument facts per program version: variance power clause, beneficiary identity (resource provider itself/affiliate), donor redirect/refund rights, time/purpose restrictions, and financial interrelation.

**Business assumptions.**

- Program documents are stored per version
- Sponsors host agency funds

**Recommended answer.** Treatments: (1) Contribution without donor restrictions - transfer instrument explicitly grants variance power, no donor redirect/refund right, no time/purpose restriction beyond advisory privileges. (2) Contribution with donor restrictions - instrument imposes time (incl. perpetual) or purpose restriction the sponsor treats as binding (variance power does not remove perpetual endowment restrictions per Boston practice). (3) Liability (agency) - resource provider names itself or affiliate as beneficiary (REGARDLESS of variance power), or provider retains redirect/revocation/refund right, or controls the recipient. (4) Equity transaction - financially interrelated entities naming themselves. Missing variance-power evidence blocks (1).

**Remaining uncertainty.** Current ASC paragraph numbers for FAS 136 content (958-605-25-24 cross-reference seen in ASU 2018-08; others unverified); sponsor-specific auditor judgments on designated funds.

**Consequences if wrong.** Misclassification of revenue vs liability; overstated net assets; Schedule D line errors.

**Conservative fallback.** Classify as liability any program where variance power evidence is missing or donor retains redirect/refund rights, pending CPA review.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Store transfer-instrument facts (structured clause flags) rather than a free-choice treatment; derive treatment by rule with CPA override and evidence. Low cost; avoids marketing-label misclassification.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor counsel/CPA review of each program's transfer-instrument wording (variance power and beneficiary terms).

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a nonprofit-established agency fund whose agreement grants variance power, when classified, then treatment is Liability.
- Given a workplace program allowing donors to redirect or refund, when classified, then treatment is Liability.
- Given a DAF program version lacking a stored variance-power clause, when a contribution posts, then posting as unrestricted contribution is blocked.
- Given a perpetual endowment gift with variance power, when classified, then treatment is With Donor Restrictions.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- New program type
- Auditor reclassification
- FASB amendment

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-ACC-001#Q1**: Classify by transfer-instrument facts: explicit variance power and no donor redirect/refund right -> contribution; donor redirect/refund right, provider-named-as-beneficiary or provider control -> liability (FAS 136 paras 12, 17).

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-INV-002 decision_outcome item (2): Says nonprofit-beneficiary agency funds are liabilities 'without granting variance power'; FAS 136 para 17(d) and Example paras 53-56 make them liabilities even when variance power is granted. → Remove the 'without variance power' qualifier.
- XID-INV-002 item (1): Implies variance power alone yields without-donor-restriction classification; Boston keeps perpetual endowments with restrictions and Fidelity has with-restriction accounts. → Variance power makes the sponsor a donee; time/purpose restrictions are separately evaluated.

### XID-INV-005 — Confirm provisional decision: Schedule D line 4 valuation basis and line 3 grant basis (approved vs paid).

**Disposition:** reasoned-interpretation · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** Schedule D Part I: line 4 = aggregate fair value of DAF assets at year end, the same measurement as the audited statements; line 3 = all distributions from DAFs including transfers to other funds of the organization, as the instructions require, on the same recognition basis as grant expense (XID-ADV-006). The UNB-XIN-ACC-006 bridge reconciles line 3/4 to GAAP and to fund statements with typed reconciling items (intra-organization transfers, recognition vs payment timing, agency funds, valuation date).

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-XIN-ACC-006, unbound:requirements/FULL-ACCOUNTING-SYSTEM.md, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-24-zero-consultation-evidence-review-01.md, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-24-question-resolutions-accounting-banking-economics.md

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Line 2: aggregate value of contributions to DAFs during the year; line 3: aggregate value of grants from DAFs, including both grants outside the organization and transfers within the organization; line 4: aggregate value at end of year. [EV-B-016, Part I lines 2-4]
- *established* — DAFs are not limited to GAAP 'funds'; exceptions include funds distributing only to a single identified organization; column (b) covers other similar funds. [EV-B-016, Part I definition, Exceptions, Column (b)]
- *interpretation* — The instructions specify no measurement basis (fair value vs cost, accrual vs paid) for lines 2-4. [EV-B-016, Part I (absence)]
- *established* — Unless instructed otherwise, the return should use the same accounting method the organization uses for its books and records. [EV-B-017, General Instructions - Accounting method]
- *established* — Parts XI-XII reconcile audited ASC 958 revenue/expenses to the return. [EV-B-016, Parts XI-XII]
- *established* — Schedule D instructions (Rev. 12/2024) are continuous-use for tax year 2024 and later until superseded. [EV-B-016, What's New]

**Supporting interpretation.** Using the books' method (accrual grant recognition; fair value for investments) for lines 2-4 follows the general accounting-method instruction and makes Schedule D reconcile to audited statements with typed bridge items.

**Competing interpretation.** Some preparers report line 3 on a paid basis, or exclude intra-organization transfers; agency funds may or may not be DAFs (single-organization funds are excepted) and may belong in column (b) or nowhere.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound produces Schedule D line data and bridge; the sponsor's preparer signs.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsor books on accrual GAAP

**Recommended answer.** Line 4 = year-end value of DAF assets on the books' basis (fair value for investments, net of DAF-attributed liabilities such as grants payable when the grant has been expensed); line 3 = grants from DAFs on the same recognition basis as grant expense, plus transfers within the organization (instruction); line 2 = contributions on the GAAP recognition basis. Classify each fund into column (a) DAF, column (b) similar fund, or neither using the statutory definition and exceptions (not GAAP labels). Bridge typed items to GAAP: intra-organization transfers, agency funds, excepted funds, recognition vs paid timing.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Whether line 4 should be gross assets or net of payables is not stated; preparer practice varies.

**Consequences if wrong.** Schedule D inconsistencies; IRS/public scrutiny of DAF payout metrics.

**Conservative fallback.** Report per preparer instruction with full bridge disclosed in Part XIII.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Provide both gross and net line-4 values and paid vs accrued line-3 values with a preparer-selected basis per tax year; low cost, avoids locking a contested basis.

**External authorization required.** Signed Form 990 by the sponsor officer; preparer acceptance of the line-mapping basis.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given $10M DAF grants expensed, of which $0.5M unpaid at year end, and $0.2M transfers between DAFs and other funds, when line 3 is produced on accrual basis, then it equals $10.2M and the bridge shows $0.5M timing vs paid.
- Given a fund that distributes only to one identified organization, when Part I is computed, then it is excluded from column (a).
- Given a tax-year form revision, when generating, then the instruction version used is recorded.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- New Schedule D revision
- Finalized §4966 regulations defining DAF terms

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-ACC-006#Q1**: Instructions say only 'aggregate value at the end of the year'; use the books' basis (fair value) and store gross assets, DAF-attributed liabilities and net so the preparer can select; default net of DAF grants payable when grant expense is accrued.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-INV-005 decision_record.rationale 'Instructions silent': Instructions are silent on measurement but not on scope: line 3 expressly includes transfers within the organization, and the general 990 instructions require book-method consistency. → Cite Part I line 3 and General Instructions accounting method.

### XID-INV-006 — Confirm provisional decision: UBIT recognition policy (accrue estimate vs recognize on filing) and fund allocation rule.

**Disposition:** reasoned-interpretation · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** United States (federal tax law; US GAAP for not-for-profit entities) · **Batch:** B (Accounting and financial reporting)

**Original provisional answer.** UBIT is accrued under ASC 740 in the period the unrelated business taxable income is earned (quarterly estimate), trued up on filing Form 990-T. The estimated tax is allocated to the fund(s) that held the income-producing asset in proportion to attributed UBTI, and reduces capacity through a recorded hold at the estimate until the true-up.

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-XIN-ACC-008, unbound:requirements/FULL-ACCOUNTING-SYSTEM.md, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv, unbound:requirements/discovery/2026-11-24-question-resolutions-accounting-banking-economics.md

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — All entities, including tax-exempt NFPs, are subject to ASC 740; UBI recognition is a tax position to evaluate. [EV-B-019, article (ASU 2009-06)]
- *established* — NFPs must disclose income tax expense and the nature of activities generating it. [EV-B-015, 958-720-50-1(c)]
- *established* — Form 990-T is required at >= $1,000 gross UBI, due the 15th day of the 5th month; estimated tax installments required if expected tax >= $500. [EV-B-018, Who Must File; When To File; estimated tax]
- *established* — Colorado Gives' audited policy: UBIT 'estimated and recognized as management and general expense in the period the income tax return is filed'. [EV-B-010, Note 2 Income Taxes]
- *established* — Fidelity may allocate UBIT arising from contributed property to the Giving Account; Vanguard pays UBIT related to certain illiquid contributions. [EV-B-007, Costs associated with contributions (and EV-B-009 Note 8)]

**Supporting interpretation.** Accrual in the period UBTI is earned follows ASC 740 current-tax principles; allocating to the generating fund mirrors Fidelity's program terms; a capacity hold prevents granting tax money.

**Competing interpretation.** Where UBIT is immaterial, audited practice (Colorado Gives) recognizes it when the return is filed; auditors accept this on materiality grounds.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound should always place the capacity hold at fund level (risk control) and let the sponsor's policy choose GL timing (accrual default; filing basis permitted if CPA-documented as immaterial).

**Business assumptions.**

- UBTI sources identifiable per fund (S-corp, K-1, debt-financed)
- Program terms permit charging tax to the fund

**Recommended answer.** Default: accrue estimated UBIT in the period earned (quarterly estimates aligned with 990-T installments), true-up on filing, allocate to generating funds pro rata to attributed UBTI where program terms allow, and hold that capacity until true-up. Permit filing-basis GL recognition only under a documented sponsor policy (immateriality), while keeping the fund-level capacity hold.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Codification paragraphs not read; allocation of shared deductions/specific deduction across funds is a tax method choice.

**Consequences if wrong.** Granting money owed as tax; misallocated tax across donors.

**Conservative fallback.** Capacity hold at estimate regardless of GL policy.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Refuse UBTI-generating assets (no S-corp/K-1 acceptance): eliminates exposure but loses complex-asset gifts (Fidelity reports $1.75B private equity securities contributed assets). Recommended: accept with hold and allocation.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor program terms permitting charge of UBIT to the fund; sponsor tax preparer's allocation method.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given policy Accrue, when Q2 closes with $20,000 UBTI attributed to fund F, then an estimated tax accrual posts in Q2 allocated to F and F's capacity is held.
- Given policy FilingBasis, when Q2 closes, then no GL accrual posts but F's capacity hold is still placed.
- Given expected annual tax >= $500, when quarter installment dates approach, then an estimated-payment task is created.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Material UBIT
- Auditor request
- Tax law change to §511-514

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-ACC-008#Q1**: ASC 740 applies; default accrual with true-up; filing-basis recognition seen in audited practice (Colorado Gives) acceptable only under documented immateriality policy; capacity hold always.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-INV-006 decision_outcome.rationale 'GAAP requires accrual of income tax': True in principle but audited practice shows filing-basis recognition (Colorado Gives FY2025) accepted when immaterial. → Make GL timing a policy with accrual default; keep capacity hold unconditional.

### RCD-028 — Confirm provisional decision: Payments: unknown-outcome resolution, check lifecycle and escheat, destination proof, NOC handling, returned funds.

**Disposition:** reasoned-interpretation · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** US: Nacha Operating Rules (contract); UCC Articles 3, 4, 4A as enacted by states; state unclaimed-property law (RUUPA/UUPA enactments) · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** Use a partner payment rail for ACH (no own ACH origination) and bank positive-pay for checks. Adopt the Unknown-resolution protocol, check lifecycle, DestinationEvidence table and return-to-fund rules. Uncashed checks are voided after 180 days, re-credited to the fund, and the charity is contacted; escheat applicability goes to counsel per state.

**Relevant requirements.** RCD-028 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/reconciliation/2026-11-23/data/decisions.csv), RCF-GRT-010, RCF-GRT-012, RCF-GRT-017, RCF-GRT-018, RCF-GRT-019, RCF-GRT-035, RCF-GRT-037, RCC-GRT-006, UNB-RCN-GRT-004/005/008/015/017 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/reconciliation/2026-11-23/data/gap-requirements.jsonl; /home/user/unbound/requirements/grants/END-TO-END-GRANT-LIFECYCLE-AND-OPERATING-MODEL.md), UNB-XIN-BNK-007 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl), RCD-047, XID-BNK-003, SPW-069 (simplification-economics/03-ENTERPRISE-WORK-INVENTORY.md)

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Originators must apply Notification of Change corrections within six banking days of receipt or before initiating the next entry, whichever is later. [EV-C-005, Notification of Change: Sections 2.12, 3.9, Appendix 5]
- *established* — From 2026-06-22 at the latest every non-consumer Originator, TPSP and TPS must operate risk-based procedures to identify entries unauthorized or authorized under False Pretenses (e.g., change controls on payee bank details), reviewed annually. [EV-C-001, Phase 2 effective dates and obligations]
- *established* — Where name and account number conflict, the beneficiary's bank may pay on the account number; a non-bank originator with notice bears the loss. Destination proof of account ownership is therefore a loss-allocation control, not just hygiene. [EV-C-016, UCC 4A-207(b),(c)]
- *established* — A bank need not, but may, pay a check over six months old; staleness does not cancel it. [EV-C-019, UCC 4-404]
- *established* — An uncashed check only suspends the underlying obligation; voiding the instrument does not discharge any obligation that exists. [EV-C-020, UCC 3-310(b)]
- *established* — RUUPA treats a holder's record of an unpaid check as prima facie evidence of an obligation, rebuttable if the check was issued without consideration or voided [not later than 90 days]/[within a reasonable time] after issuance for a valid business reason recorded contemporaneously. [EV-C-021, RUUPA Sec. 1005(a),(c)(6),(c)(8)]
- *interpretation* — Customers must examine statements promptly; check-fraud losses are allocated by preclusion and comparative fault with a one-year bar; positive pay is a contractual, not statutory, control. [EV-C-018, UCC 4-406(c)-(f)]
- *established* — Published national-sponsor void rules are 90 days with return to the giving account; none states an escheat treatment. [EV-C-044, P-NPP-02]

**Supporting interpretation.** The partner-rail and positive-pay direction is sound and consistent with Nacha role definitions. The 180-day void is weaker than needed: a void within 90 days of issuance with a contemporaneous business-reason record fits the narrowest RUUPA bracket and matches Fidelity/GiveClear/BlueGive practice, and is earlier than any state's dormancy period, so the obligation question can be resolved before escheat timing arises. Automatic re-credit is unsafe because voiding does not by itself extinguish the underlying grant obligation (UCC 3-310) and some states (e.g., Delaware, California per prior research) list no void defence.

**Competing interpretation.** A grant is a gift; a sponsor's own check delivered as a gift is arguably revocable until paid, so there is no fixed obligation to escheat and re-credit after any void is lawful. This is plausible under RUUPA Sec. 1005(c)(6) and Delaware 1175(b)(6), but it depends on the grant terms (a grant letter or agreement may create an enforceable obligation) and on each state's text.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound does not hold funds; it must model check lifecycle states (issued, outstanding, stop/void requested, void confirmed by bank issue file, replaced, cancelled-grant, escheat-candidate, reported) and evidence for each sponsor-owned account, and must not post a fund re-credit from a void alone.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsor holds the disbursement account and the ODFI/positive-pay agreements.
- Grant letters do not promise payment irrespective of acceptance (to be checked per sponsor).
- Charity contact attempts and ACH re-offer are operationally feasible within 90 days.

**Recommended answer.** Confirm with changes: (1) partner rail or sponsor-originated ACH via the sponsor's ODFI, never Unbound-originated; (2) positive pay (payee-name where available) on every check account with daily issue files including voids; (3) NOC corrections applied automatically within 6 banking days or before the next entry, whichever is later, with change control because NOCs alter payee bank data; (4) destination evidence (account-ownership verification or charity-signed bank letter) before first electronic payment and after any bank-detail change; (5) default void at 90 days (sponsor-configurable, never later than the shortest applicable state bracket) with a contemporaneous recorded reason, bank-confirmed void/stop, charity notice and ACH re-offer; (6) re-credit to the fund only via the RCD-047/XID-BNK-003 disposition step (grant cancelled under its terms and state-table outcome 'no holder obligation'), otherwise hold as an outstanding payable until reissue or escheat reporting.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Per-state treatment of gift checks; whether each sponsor's grant letter creates an enforceable obligation; bank-specific positive-pay and void file capabilities.

**Consequences if wrong.** Unreported unclaimed property (interest and penalties in audits, typically multi-year lookbacks); duplicate payments after re-issue; check fraud losses shifted to the sponsor under bank agreements if positive pay is not used.

**Conservative fallback.** Never re-credit after void; hold voided-check amounts as payables to the charity until reissued or reported to the state of the charity's address (else sponsor's state of incorporation).

**Alternative business or architecture design.** ACH-first/check-on-request (SPW-069) or a networked rail (Chariot Disbursements, PayPal Grant Payments) shrinks the check population: cost = partner fees/integration; friction = charity enrolment; exposure = far fewer uncashed instruments and escheat events; lost opportunity = charities that will not enrol still need checks.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor's ODFI origination agreement and positive-pay service agreement (bank approval); sponsor board/CFO adoption of the void and disposition policy.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given an NOC (COR/C0x) is received for a payee, When processed, Then the corrected data is staged for change-controlled approval and applied before the next entry or within 6 banking days, whichever is later, with audit evidence.
- Given a check outstanding 90 days (configurable), When the void job runs, Then a void record with reason, bank void/stop confirmation and charity notice is stored and the amount moves to 'voided-pending-disposition', not to the fund balance.
- Given a voided check, When re-credit is requested, Then it is blocked unless the RCD-047 disposition record (grant cancelled and state-table outcome) exists.
- Given a new or changed payee bank account, When an electronic payment is scheduled, Then destination evidence of account ownership exists and is less than the configured age.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Rail selection (CAP-25) or sponsor bank change
- State adopts/repeals a void or B2B provision
- Nacha changes NOC or reinitiation rules
- Unclaimed-property audit inquiry

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- RCD-028 decided_option: States uncashed checks are 'voided after 180 days, re-credited to the fund'. Voiding does not extinguish the underlying obligation (UCC 3-310(b)); RUUPA's narrowest void bracket is 90 days; automatic re-credit conflicts with RCD-047. → Void at 90 days default with contemporaneous record; re-credit only through the RCD-047 disposition step.
- RCD-028 recommendation 'partner rail (no own ACH origination)': Even with a partner rail the sponsor is normally the Originator of record under its ODFI agreement; 'no own ACH origination' is accurate only for Unbound. → State: Unbound originates nothing; the sponsor (or a networked rail acting as the sponsor's agent) originates.

### RCD-042 — Confirm provisional decision: Unknown payment that cannot obtain NotReceived evidence

**Disposition:** business-policy-decision · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** US: UCC Article 4A (wires, business ACH credits); Nacha Operating Rules; UCC Articles 3/4 (checks) · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** After the final aging tier (default 30 days), two independent treasury approvers may resolve it by recorded decision, with a duplicate-risk acceptance and a recovery plan.

**Relevant requirements.** RCD-042 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/reconciliation/2026-11-23/data/decisions.csv), UNB-RCN-WF-022 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/ENTERPRISE-GAP-REGISTER-AND-CROSS-DOMAIN-SCENARIOS.md), RCF-WF-003, simplification-economics/05-HUMAN-INTERVENTION-TAXONOMY.md (NotReceived/Unknown row)

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — A sender's obligation is excused if the funds transfer is not completed by acceptance at the beneficiary's bank, and amounts paid must be refunded with interest. [EV-C-014, UCC 4A-402(c),(d)]
- *established* — On acceptance the beneficiary's bank must pay and notify the beneficiary by the next funds-transfer business day; acceptance is a determinable bank event. [EV-C-015, UCC 4A-404(a),(b)]
- *established* — Article 4A governs business-to-business funds transfers not governed by EFTA. [EV-C-012, UCC 4A-108]
- *established* — On PayPal Grant Payments an unaccepted grant expires on a grantmaker-set date and the grantmaker resends by another method; status is reported by the rail. [EV-C-039, help883 expiration paragraph]

**Supporting interpretation.** For wires and ACH credits the outcome is never truly unknowable: the ODFI can trace settlement, return, or beneficiary-bank acceptance, and an incomplete transfer is refundable by law (4A-402). 'Unknown' is therefore an evidence-gathering state; the two-approver resolution is a business control for the residual case (charity disputes receipt despite bank confirmation, or rail status unavailable). Aging alone is not evidence of non-receipt.

**Competing interpretation.** Some treat a long-unresolved payment as presumptively failed and re-send; this risks duplicate grants that are hard to recover from charities.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound workflow and evidence model; the sponsor's treasury takes the decision and the bank supplies trace evidence.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsor's ODFI provides trace/inquiry service and Fed/ACH trace data within days.
- Rail partners expose status APIs.

**Recommended answer.** Confirm with refinement: before the final tier, require a bank trace or rail status request (ODFI trace for ACH, Fed IMAD/OMAD and beneficiary-bank confirmation for wires, paid-item/issue file for checks). Classify outcomes: Settled-and-accepted (=Received unless charity proves misapplication), Returned/Rejected (=NotReceived), Not settled (=NotReceived, refund under 4A-402), Unobtainable. Only 'Unobtainable' after 30 days goes to two independent approvers who record duplicate-risk acceptance and recovery plan; no automatic retry. For checks, a reissue requires a confirmed stop/void of the original first.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Bank-specific trace SLAs; rail partners' status semantics.

**Consequences if wrong.** Duplicate grants (recovery from charities is slow and reputationally costly) or permanently stuck grants.

**Conservative fallback.** Never reissue an electronic payment while the original is not confirmed returned or unsettled; never reissue a check before stop/void confirmation.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Use rails with acceptance-based delivery (PayPal Grant Payments accept/decline; Chariot deposit to charity-owned account) where status is explicit: cost = rail dependence; friction = charity enrolment; exposure = unknown state nearly eliminated; lost opportunity = none material.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor CFO approval of aging tiers and delegation of authority for the two approvers.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given an electronic payment in Unknown, When the first aging tier is reached, Then a bank/rail trace request is logged automatically.
- Given trace shows settlement and beneficiary-bank acceptance, When charity claims non-receipt, Then the payment is classified Received and routed to the charity to investigate with its bank, not reissued.
- Given status Unobtainable after the final tier, When resolution is attempted, Then two distinct approvers (neither the initiator) record duplicate-risk acceptance and recovery plan before any reissue.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Bank or rail change
- Observed duplicate-payment incidents
- CFO revises aging tiers

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- RCD-042: Implies NotReceived evidence may be impossible to obtain for electronic payments; UCC 4A-402/404 and ODFI trace make the outcome determinable in nearly all cases. → Add mandatory bank/rail trace step before the final tier; reserve two-approver resolution for 'Unobtainable'.

### RCD-047 — Confirm provisional decision: Voided check: escheat versus re-credit

**Disposition:** reasoned-interpretation · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** US states (RUUPA 2016 enactments, UUPA 1995 and older-act states, non-uniform states e.g. DE, CA, NY, MA); federal priority rules (Texas v. New Jersey) · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** An escheat determination step precedes any re-credit; re-credit only where counsel's per-state rule says no escheat duty applies.

**Relevant requirements.** RCD-047 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/reconciliation/2026-11-23/data/decisions.csv), UNB-RCN-GRT-005 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/reconciliation/2026-11-23/data/gap-requirements.jsonl), RCD-028, RCF-WF-003, XID-BNK-003, UNB-XIN-BNK-007, UNB-XIN-NPG-011

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — RUUPA: debt of a business association presumed abandoned three years after the obligation arises. [EV-C-021, RUUPA Sec. 201(4)]
- *established* — RUUPA: holder may rebut the prima facie unpaid-check presumption by showing issuance without consideration or a timely void for a valid business reason recorded contemporaneously. [EV-C-021, RUUPA Sec. 1005(c)(6),(8)]
- *established* — Delaware lists the without-consideration rebuttal but not a void rebuttal; California's 3-year catch-all and Massachusetts c.200A s.5 have no such defence in the sections read; NY's outstanding-check provision covers checks for goods or services. [EV-C-025, Section 1.1 table (prior pass, statutes marked fetched there)]
- *interpretation* — Voiding a check does not itself discharge an underlying obligation. [EV-C-020, UCC 3-310(b)]

**Supporting interpretation.** The escheat-determination-before-re-credit rule is correct and can be largely automated: the disposition depends on (a) whether an obligation survives (grant cancelled under its terms before payment?), (b) the priority state (charity's address state, else sponsor's state of incorporation), and (c) that state's rebuttals (no consideration; timely void). Where (a) is 'no obligation' and the state recognises either rebuttal, re-credit is supportable; otherwise hold and report at dormancy (typically 3 years for checks/debts; Delaware catch-all 5 years).

**Competing interpretation.** Treat every outstanding grant check as reportable regardless of void (most conservative; RUUPA 1005(a) prima facie rule); or treat all gift checks as non-property (aggressive).

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound implements the jurisdiction table and disposition workflow; sponsor (holder) makes and files the determination.

**Business assumptions.**

- Charity's last-known address is in the sponsor's records (IRS BMF/990 address).
- Sponsor's state of incorporation is recorded.

**Recommended answer.** Confirm. Implement a 'VoidDisposition' record: inputs = grant cancellation status and grant-letter terms, priority state, state rule row (dormancy period, no-consideration rebuttal Y/N, void rebuttal Y/N and window, B2B exemption scope), contemporaneous void reason. Output = Re-credit permitted / Hold as payable / Report. Default when any input is unknown = Hold and report at dormancy. The state rows are populated from statute text (RUUPA enactments first), not from counsel opinion; counsel review is optional quality assurance.

**Remaining uncertainty.** State-by-state enactment of RUUPA 1005 brackets; whether a 'grant' is a debt of the sponsor in states without rebuttal provisions; absence of any enforcement history on DAF grant checks.

**Consequences if wrong.** Under-reporting: back reporting, interest and penalties at audit; over-reporting: charitable funds escheated unnecessarily (recoverable by the charity from the state).

**Conservative fallback.** Hold and report every voided grant check amount to the priority state at dormancy.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Prevent the question: void at <=90 days with contemporaneous record, cancel the grant under explicit grant-letter terms ('grant lapses if not negotiated within 90 days'), and re-offer by ACH; cost = grant-letter wording change; friction = low; exposure = reduced to states with no rebuttal; lost opportunity = none.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor board/CFO adoption of the disposition policy and grant-letter lapse clause; the state determination itself is the sponsor's as holder (no state approval needed unless filing a VDA).

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a voided grant check, When disposition runs, Then the record shows priority state, state rule version, rebuttal used and evidence, and outcome.
- Given any missing input, When disposition runs, Then outcome is Hold (never Re-credit).
- Given outcome Report, When the state's report date arrives, Then the item appears in the sponsor's unclaimed-property report extract for that state.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- State statute change
- Unclaimed-property audit or VDA
- Grant-letter terms change

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- RCD-047 decision_owner/validation 'Counsel per state': Most of the state table is answerable from statute text; framing it as counsel-only blocks implementation. → Populate from statutes (RUUPA 1005, state equivalents); counsel review optional; default Hold where unknown.

### SPD-CMP-003 — Release-1 grant-payment rail: sponsor-originated ACH, or a networked rail partner (Chariot, PayPal Grant Payments) as the sponsor's agent?

**Disposition:** business-policy-decision · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** US · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

- *established* — NPT and Vanguard use PayPal Grant Payments; Chariot Disbursements used by GiveClear, Daffy and others. [EV-C-045, rails table]

**Supporting interpretation.** Both rails are used by major sponsors; funds flow from sponsor to rail partner (a regulated bank/money transmitter), not through Unbound.

**Competing interpretation.** Sponsor-originated ACH is cheaper but requires charity bank-detail collection and verification.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Adapter strategy.

**Business assumptions.**

**Disposition:** empirical-validation-required · **Confidence:** high · **Jurisdiction:** US: Nacha file format · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

- *established* — Trace number uniquely identifies each entry within batch and file, assigned by the ODFI. [EV-C-041, Entry Detail field 11]

### UNB-XIN-PAY-001#Q1 — 2-year retention figure for Nacha authorizations is general industry knowledge (unverified in this pass); confirm in Nacha rules.

**Disposition:** established-by-authoritative-evidence · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** US: Nacha Operating Rules; Regulation E · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** (requirement) capture consumer authorization with amount, frequency, start, entity, revocation; deliver copy; notify varying amounts; retain 2 years.

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-XIN-PAY-001 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl), /home/user/unbound/requirements/open-questions.csv

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Originator must retain the authorization (or reproducible record) for two years from termination or revocation. [EV-C-009, Nacha Rules Subsection 2.3.2.7 per Nacha doc (search text)]
- *established* — Burden of proof of authorization is on the Originator. [EV-C-005, Authorization]
- *established* — Reg E: written signed/similarly authenticated authorization with copy; 10-day notice of varying amounts. [EV-C-011, 1005.10(b),(d)]

**Supporting interpretation.** Two years runs from termination/revocation, not from signature; practical design retains for authorization lifetime + 2 years (plus longer for the 2-year consumer warranty-claim tail in EV-C-006).

**Competing interpretation.** Some sources cite 2.3.2.5 rather than 2.3.2.7.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Retention policy for donor debit authorizations.

**Business assumptions.**

- none recorded

**Recommended answer.** Confirmed: retain each authorization for its active life plus two years after termination/revocation; recommend lifetime + 2 years + margin aligned with warranty-claim tail (2 years from settlement of last entry).

**Remaining uncertainty.** Exact subsection number; rulebook not read first-hand.

**Consequences if wrong.** Inability to defend unauthorized claims.

**Conservative fallback.** Retain lifetime + 3 years.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** n/a

**External authorization required.** none

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given an authorization revoked on date D, Then it is not purgeable before D + 2 years and later of last entry settlement + 2 years.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Nacha rule change

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- none

### UNB-XIN-PAY-005#Q1 — Exact reinitiation count/window to be confirmed from Nacha rules text (180 days/two times is unverified in this pass).

**Disposition:** established-by-authoritative-evidence · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** US: Nacha Operating Rules · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** (requirement) return-rate monitoring at 0.5%/3%/15%; reinitiation only for R01/R09.

**Relevant requirements.** UNB-XIN-PAY-005 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl), /home/user/unbound/requirements/open-questions.csv

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Reinitiation permitted only for (a) R01/R09 returns, (b) stop-payment returns separately authorized by the Receiver after the return, or (c) after corrective action remedying the reason for return; separate batch, 'RETRY PYMT', identical Company Name/ID/Amount. [EV-C-007, Subsection 2.12.4]
- *established* — R01/R09: at most two reinitiations within 180 days of the original settlement date; R10 available for violations (rule effective 2015-09-18). [EV-C-008, secondary summaries]
- *established* — Unauthorized return rate threshold 0.5%. [EV-C-005, Returns]

**Supporting interpretation.** The requirement 'reinitiation only for R01/R09' is narrower than the rules (which also allow stop-payment with new authorization and corrective-action reinitiation); narrower is compliant and simpler for a DAF sponsor.

**Competing interpretation.** Allow corrective-action reinitiation (e.g., after R03 with corrected account from a new authorization) - but Nacha guidance treats a new authorization as a new entry, not reinitiation.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Donor debit retry logic.

**Business assumptions.**

- none recorded

**Recommended answer.** R01/R09: max two reinitiations within 180 days of original settlement, Company Entry Description 'RETRY PYMT', separate batch, same Company Name/ID/Amount. Keep the requirement's narrower scope; permit R08 reinitiation only with a new post-return donor authorization. Note the 2021 Nacha material reaffirmed these rules; no 2026 change found.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Rulebook text not read first-hand.

**Consequences if wrong.** R10 returns and ODFI enforcement.

**Conservative fallback.** One retry only.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** No automatic retries; donor re-initiates gift.

**External authorization required.** none

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given an R01 return, When retry scheduled, Then count<=2, date<=original settlement+180 days, description='RETRY PYMT'.
- Given an R05/R07/R10/R29 return, Then no reinitiation is possible.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Nacha rule change

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- none

### XID-BNK-001 — Will the sponsor accept card contributions, and with what maximum per gift and contest threshold?

**Original provisional answer.** Accept cards only for D2C/embedded/high-volume models; cap per gift (e.g., $10,000 illustrative); contest disputes >= $1,000 illustrative.

**Relevant requirements.** XID-BNK-001 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/open-questions.csv), UNB-XIN-PAY-003, XID-BNK-006, simplification matrix: card intake excluded from R1

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — 120 (up to 540) day chargeback windows; Visa response 9/18 days, Mastercard 40. [EV-C-033, table]

**Supporting interpretation.** Business policy; exposure tail 120-540 days.

**Competing interpretation.** 

**Applicability to Unbound.** Feature flag.

**Business assumptions.**

- Card dispute rates low.

**Recommended answer.** Defer cards from Release 1; when enabled: per-gift cap configurable ($10,000 default), contest threshold configurable ($1,000 default) given response deadlines, acknowledgment issued after settlement and corrected on chargeback.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Acquirer terms.

**Consequences if wrong.** Losses or donor friction.

**Conservative fallback.** No cards.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Use a third-party DAF-giving platform as merchant of record (shifts chargeback risk, but the gift becomes to that charity).

**External authorization required.** Acquirer merchant agreement.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a card dispute notice, Then response deadline computed per network is tracked.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Sponsor demand

### XID-BNK-002 — Confirm provisional decision: Is a donor-covered processing fee part of the contribution stated on the acknowledgment?

**Disposition:** reasoned-interpretation · **Confidence:** high · **Jurisdiction:** US federal tax (IRC 170, Treas. Reg. 1.170A-1) · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** When a donor elects to cover processing fees, the contribution and the acknowledgment are the gross amount charged. The fund is credited gross and the processing fee is shown as a separate fund expense line, so the fund nets the intended amount.

**Relevant requirements.** XID-BNK-002 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv), UNB-XIN-PAY-006 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl), UNB-RCN-FIN-001, UNB-XIN-PAY-006#Q1 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/open-questions.csv), requirements/accounting/CANONICAL-CHART-AND-POSTING-RULES.md

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — The acknowledgment must state the amount of cash contributed and whether goods or services were provided. [EV-C-030, IRC 170(f)(8)(B)(i)]
- *established* — Pub 1771 lists 'the amount of any monetary contribution' and a statement that no goods or services were provided. [EV-C-027, p. 2, items 1-6]
- *established* — A DAF acknowledgment must also state that the sponsor has exclusive legal control over the assets contributed. [EV-C-030, IRC 170(f)(18)(B)]
- *interpretation* — A payment is reduced only by the FMV of goods or services received from the donee; the donor receives none for fee coverage. [EV-C-029, 1.170A-1(h)(1),(2)]
- *interpretation* — Industry practice: voluntary donor-elected fee coverage paid to the charity is acknowledged at the full amount; mandatory fees are not gifts. [EV-C-032, search snippets]

**Supporting interpretation.** If the donor voluntarily pays the gross amount to the sponsor (sponsor is merchant of record and the processor deducts its fee from settlement), the donor has transferred the gross in cash to a 170(c) organization with nothing in return; the processor fee is the sponsor's cost. The acknowledgment amount is the gross. No IRS ruling addresses donor-covered fees specifically; the conclusion follows directly from 170(f)(8) and 1.170A-1(h).

**Competing interpretation.** If the 'fee' is paid to a third party (platform tip, processor convenience fee charged by a non-charity, mandatory fee) it is not a contribution to the sponsor and must be excluded. Some receipts show net amounts in error.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound must record gross, fee, net settlement and acknowledgment amount separately, and must know the merchant-of-record identity to apply the rule.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsor is merchant of record (or processor settles gross-minus-fee to the sponsor).
- Fee coverage is optional and disclosed as part of the gift.

**Recommended answer.** Confirm with a condition: when fee coverage is voluntary and the sponsor (a 170(c) organization) is merchant of record, acknowledgment amount = gross charged; fund credited gross; processing fee posted as an expense (fund-level only if the fee schedule discloses pass-through, else sponsor operating). If any part is paid to a non-charity (platform fee, mandatory convenience fee) exclude it from the acknowledgment and show it separately.

**Remaining uncertainty.** No IRS guidance specific to fee coverage; card-network surcharge rules may restrict labeling.

**Consequences if wrong.** Over-stated acknowledgment (donor deduction overstated by ~3%); low magnitude.

**Conservative fallback.** Acknowledge gross only where merchant of record is the sponsor; otherwise acknowledge net received.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Do not offer fee coverage; absorb fees or charge a disclosed fund fee: cost = sponsor bears ~2-3% card cost; friction none; exposure eliminated; lost opportunity = fee recovery revenue.

**External authorization required.** none

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a card gift of $100 with $3 voluntary fee coverage and sponsor as merchant of record, When the acknowledgment is generated, Then it states $103 cash, no goods or services, and exclusive legal control.
- Given a payment component paid to a non-170(c) party, When the acknowledgment is generated, Then that component is excluded and itemised separately.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- IRS guidance on fee coverage
- Change of merchant of record (processor or platform becomes MoR)

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-PAY-006#Q1**: Yes when voluntary and paid to the sponsor as merchant of record: the gross is the contribution and acknowledgment amount; amounts paid to non-charities are excluded.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-BNK-002 authority: Cites 170(f)(8),(f)(18) without the merchant-of-record condition; decision silently assumes sponsor receives the gross. → Add condition: applies only when the sponsor is merchant of record and fee coverage is voluntary.

### XID-BNK-003 — Confirm provisional decision: Is a voided/stale DAF grant check property owed to the charity for unclaimed-property purposes, and which state rules (incl. B2B exemptions) apply?

**Disposition:** reasoned-interpretation · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** US states; federal common-law priority (Texas v. New Jersey, 1965); Federal Disposition Act not applicable to sponsor-drawn checks · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** An outstanding uncashed grant check is presumed property owed to the payee charity and reportable under the unclaimed-property law of the payee's last-known address state, else the sponsor's state of incorporation (priority rule). Before dormancy: two outreach attempts and an offer to reissue by ACH. The sponsor may void and re-credit the fund only where (a) the grant is cancelled before the check is cashed and the charity is notified, and (b) the applicable state table records that cancellation defeats the holder obligation (B2B or charity exemptions). Otherwise report.

**Relevant requirements.** XID-BNK-003 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv), UNB-XIN-BNK-007 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl), UNB-XIN-NPG-011, UNB-RCN-GRT-005, REG-045, OB-45, discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/industry/grt.md (Unclaimed property row)

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Priority: state of the owner's last-known address in the holder's records; then the holder's domicile (state of incorporation). [EV-C-021, RUUPA Secs. 302-304 and comment restating Texas v. New Jersey]
- *established* — Texas v. New Jersey primary/secondary rules. [EV-C-022, 379 U.S. 674 (not fetched)]
- *interpretation* — Delaware v. Pennsylvania (2023) placed MoneyGram agent and teller's checks under the Federal Disposition Act; ordinary sponsor-drawn checks remain under the common-law rules. [EV-C-023, holding (search results)]
- *established* — Nonprofit holders are covered ('whether or not for profit'). [EV-C-025, Section 1.2 item 1; RUUPA Sec. 102]
- *established* — Rebuttals: issued without consideration; voided within the bracketed window with contemporaneous record. [EV-C-021, Sec. 1005(c)(6),(8)]
- *established* — About 15 states had B2B exemptions; some repealed (Illinois; Arizona reported 2026); RUUPA requires a state to define B2B expressly to keep it. [EV-C-026, search snippets]
- *established* — RUUPA comment confirms 15 states had B2B exemptions and how a state keeps one. [EV-C-021, Sec. 102 comment, Business-to-business]

**Supporting interpretation.** A B2B exemption is the wrong primary lever: many exemptions require both parties to be business associations in an ongoing relationship for goods or services (e.g., Ohio), which a charitable grant is not. The stronger statutory lever is the no-consideration rebuttal (gift) plus a timely void with contemporaneous record, available in RUUPA states and (consideration only) Delaware. Where neither applies, report.

**Competing interpretation.** A grant check is a completed gift and therefore a fixed obligation to the charity once delivered; on that view every outstanding check is reportable whatever the void.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Jurisdiction table and disposition engine in Unbound; sponsor is the holder and filer.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsor concentration states: identify from sponsor roster (DE, CA, NY, MA, IL, PA, TX likely).
- Charity addresses from IRS records.

**Recommended answer.** Confirm the provisional decision with three changes: (1) the primary state-table attribute is the no-consideration and timely-void rebuttal, B2B exemptions are secondary and default to 'not applicable to grants' unless the statute text covers non-commercial payments; (2) void window default 90 days; (3) jurisdiction table method: one row per state with citation, dormancy period, rebuttals, B2B scope, report date, last verified date; populate sponsor-concentration states first from statute text; unknown rows default to Report.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Exact enactment status of RUUPA 1005 brackets per state; no enforcement precedent on DAF grant checks.

**Consequences if wrong.** Audit exposure with interest/penalties, or unnecessary escheat of charitable funds.

**Conservative fallback.** Report all uncashed grant checks to the priority state at dormancy.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Electronic-first grants (ACH/rail) with lapse clause in grant letter: removes most instruments from scope at low cost; lost opportunity minimal.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor CFO/board adoption of policy; no regulator authorization needed (VDA only if past non-compliance found).

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a charity address in state S, When priority is computed, Then S is selected; Given no address, Then the sponsor's state of incorporation is selected.
- Given state row lacks verified status, When disposition runs, Then outcome is Report.
- Given a state row, Then it carries a statute citation and last-verified date.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- State law change
- Audit
- Sponsor relocates incorporation

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-BNK-007#Q1**: Voiding the instrument does not by itself extinguish the obligation; keep a payable or hold until the disposition record shows cancellation plus a state rebuttal, else report.
- **RCD-047**: Confirmed: escheat determination precedes re-credit; see RCD-047 dossier.
- **UNB-XIN-NPG-011#Q1**: No blanket B2B exemption; most B2B exemptions cover commercial goods/services relationships and should be treated as inapplicable to grants unless statute text says otherwise; rely on no-consideration/void rebuttals where enacted.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-BNK-003 authority 'Texas v. New Jersey (general knowledge)': Unverified citation. → Now supported by RUUPA Sec. 302 comment (fetched); also note Delaware v. Pennsylvania (2023) does not alter rules for sponsor-drawn checks.
- XID-BNK-003 decision (b) 'B2B or charity exemptions': No general charity exemption exists; B2B exemptions are a weak fit for gifts. → Replace with 'no-consideration or timely-void rebuttal recognised by the state'.

### XID-BNK-005 — Confirm provisional decision: Who is Originator of record and does any vendor hold funds (TPS/money transmission)?

**Disposition:** established-by-authoritative-evidence · **Confidence:** high · **Jurisdiction:** US: Nacha Operating Rules (contractual); state money-transmission laws (not reached if no funds handled) · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** The sponsor is the Originator of record on sponsor-titled accounts under its own ODFI agreement. The platform operator is a Third-Party Service Provider that transmits files on the sponsor's instruction; it is not a Third-Party Sender, holds no funds and has no FBO or omnibus account (XRJ-BNK-005 confirmed).

**Relevant requirements.** XID-BNK-005 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv), UNB-XIN-BNK-001 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl), UNB-XIN-REG-001, RR-UNB-PAY-008-r1, OB-43, UNB-XIN-OTH-003

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — A TPS acts for an Originator without a direct Originator-ODFI agreement; a TPSP provides processing services and may or may not transmit entries. [EV-C-003, Definitions]
- *established* — Nested TPS: a TPS with an agreement with another TPS; each TPS must perform its own risk assessment and audit. [EV-C-004, Definition; Risk Assessment]
- *established* — From 2026-06-22 TPSPs (and Originators) must have risk-based fraud monitoring procedures reviewed annually. [EV-C-001, Phase 2]
- *established* — TPSPs are subject to annual Rules Compliance Audit for functions performed for a Participating DFI or TPS (as described by Nacha page). [EV-C-003, Audit paragraph]

**Supporting interpretation.** With a direct sponsor-ODFI origination agreement and sponsor-titled accounts, Unbound is a TPSP under Nacha definitions, not a TPS. Because Unbound never receives or holds funds, money-transmission analysis is not triggered (interpretation; not researched in depth here). The decision should also record Unbound's own Nacha-derived obligations as a TPSP: fraud monitoring (Phase 2), data security, and cooperation with the ODFI's audits, imposed through the sponsor/ODFI contracts.

**Competing interpretation.** If Unbound ever contracts with an ODFI on behalf of multiple sponsors (multi-tenant origination), it becomes a TPS (registration, audits, warranties) and possibly a money transmitter if funds pass through its accounts.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Direct: defines Unbound's role and contract terms.

**Business assumptions.**

- Each sponsor signs its own ODFI origination agreement.
- No Unbound-titled settlement or FBO account.

**Recommended answer.** Confirm. Add: (1) Unbound is a TPSP and accepts TPSP obligations by contract (fraud monitoring per Nacha Phase 2, security, audit cooperation); (2) role table per program records Originator, ODFI, TPSP(s), any TPS/rail partner and authorization holder; (3) prohibit any configuration in which Unbound signs an origination agreement or holds funds without a new decision.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Exact text of each sponsor's ODFI agreement; whether rail partners (Chariot, PayPal) act as TPS for the sponsor.

**Consequences if wrong.** Unregistered TPS status (ODFI termination, fines passed through), or money-transmitter licensing exposure.

**Conservative fallback.** Bank-direct file upload by sponsor staff with Unbound only generating files.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Sponsor uploads Unbound-generated files to its bank portal itself: cost = manual step; friction = moderate; exposure = Unbound out of transmission path; lost opportunity = straight-through processing.

**External authorization required.** Each sponsor's executed ODFI origination agreement naming Unbound as TPSP (or permitting TPSP use).

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a program configuration, When ACH is enabled, Then the role table names the sponsor as Originator with a referenced ODFI agreement ID and Unbound as TPSP.
- Given any attempt to configure an Unbound-titled settlement account, Then configuration is rejected.
- Given a year passes, When the compliance calendar runs, Then the TPSP fraud-monitoring procedure review is evidenced.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Unbound offers pooled origination
- Nacha TPS rule changes

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-BNK-001#Q1**: Record per program: Originator = sponsor; ODFI = sponsor's bank; TPSP = Unbound (and any processor); TPS only if a rail partner contracts with the ODFI on the sponsor's behalf; authorization holder = sponsor.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-BNK-005 authority 'Nacha web pages, partly search summaries': Now verified by fetched Nacha pages; but record omits that TPSPs have their own 2026 fraud-monitoring obligation. → Cite EV-C-003/004/001; add TPSP obligations.

### XID-BNK-006 — Confirm provisional decision: Card reversal window and provisional credit policy for cards and business ACH.

**Disposition:** business-policy-decision · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** US: Nacha Operating Rules; Regulation E (consumer accounts); UCC 4A (business credits); card network rules via acquirer agreement · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** Contributions are recognized as revenue when the funds settle to the sponsor (unconditional gift received); reversibility affects availability, not recognition. Availability for grants: ACH consumer debit - available after 5 banking days, with exposure to late unauthorized returns capped by a per-donor provisional limit; business (CCD) debit - 3 banking days; card - after settlement, up to a per-donor provisional limit, remainder after 60 days; wire/check cleared - on settlement. Per-donor provisional limit defaults to $0 for donors with < 90 days history and $10,000 thereafter (illustrative, sponsor-configurable). A chargeback or return after acceptance reverses contribution revenue against the fund to the extent of its balance; any shortfall is a sponsor operating loss, never a donor receivable.

**Relevant requirements.** XID-BNK-006 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv), RR-TVR-CON-001-r1, OD-04, UNB-XIN-PAY-003 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl), UNB-RCN-FIN-013, XIC-BNK-001

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Consumer SEC codes carry an extended return right of 60 days for unauthorized entries; corporate codes two banking days. [EV-C-005, SEC Code Usage]
- *established* — Beyond return windows, RDFI warranty claims run up to two years (consumer, after first 95 days) and one year (non-consumer) from settlement. [EV-C-006, Consumer/Non-Consumer paragraphs]
- *established* — Regulation E protects only consumer accounts of natural persons. [EV-C-010, 1005.2(b)(1),(e)]
- *established* — Card chargebacks: generally 120 days, up to 540 days in some cases. [EV-C-033, timeframe table]
- *established* — Nacha has proposed an additional shortened return timeframe (listed effective 2028-09-15), not yet rule. [EV-C-046, RFC (search)]

**Supporting interpretation.** Availability windows are a business risk policy, not legal limits; the legal tail is longer than the windows (60 days consumer ACH, 1-2 years warranty claims, 120-540 days cards). The decision correctly separates recognition from availability and caps exposure with per-donor limits; it should state the residual tail explicitly and size limits against it. For DAF gifts granted out quickly, losses after grant are sponsor losses (cannot claw back grants).

**Competing interpretation.** Hold card gifts 120 days (research-pass default): near-zero loss but significant donor friction.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound computes availability and exposure; sponsor CFO sets limits.

**Business assumptions.**

- Dispute rates for charitable card gifts are low (unverified).
- Sponsor accepts operating loss for post-grant reversals.

**Recommended answer.** Confirm with corrections: document that consumer ACH unauthorized returns run to 60 days and warranty claims to 2 years, CCD returns 2 banking days and warranty claims 1 year, cards 120 (max 540) days; availability defaults as provisional decision; exposure cap = per-donor limit across all reversible un-matured amounts; chargeback/return reverses contribution against the fund to extent of balance; shortfall sponsor loss; acknowledgment amended/voided on reversal.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Acquirer-specific terms and reason-code windows; actual dispute rates.

**Consequences if wrong.** Sponsor operating losses when reversed gifts were already granted.

**Conservative fallback.** No grant availability for card gifts until 120 days and consumer ACH until 60 days.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Exclude cards from Release 1 (already proposed in simplification matrix) and accept ACH/wire/check/securities only: cost = lost small-donor convenience; exposure = card tail removed.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor CFO approval of limits; acquirer merchant agreement for card acceptance.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a consumer ACH gift, When availability is computed, Then exposure remains tracked for 60 days and counted against the donor limit.
- Given a chargeback after a grant, When posted, Then the fund is debited to its balance and the shortfall to sponsor operating loss, and the acknowledgment is corrected.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Observed loss rates
- Nacha shortened-return rule adoption
- Acquirer contract

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-PAY-003#Q1**: Network baseline 120 days (up to 540) for Visa and Mastercard; merchant response 9/18 days Visa, 40 days Mastercard per Adyen; version by acquirer and reason code.
- **RR-TVR-CON-001-r1#Q1**: Same as UNB-XIN-PAY-003#Q1; store per network, reason code and acquirer with effective dates.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-BNK-006 authority 'Visa dispute windows 120/540 days (search summary)': Now supported by acquirer documentation (Adyen, fetched); Nacha warranty-claim tail (1-2 years) was omitted. → Add EV-C-006 tail and EV-C-033.

### XID-BNK-007 — Which bank activity feed (BAI2/camt.053/API) per sponsor bank, and daily latency?

**Disposition:** empirical-validation-required · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** n/a (technical standards) · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** Require next-morning prior-day feed minimum; intraday where available.

- *established* — BTRS (X9.121) succeeds BAI2; camt.052 intraday, camt.053 end-of-day, camt.054 notifications. [EV-C-036, search]

**Supporting interpretation.** Prior-day feeds are insufficient for spendable capacity (per adversarial review); use reservations plus intraday where available.

**Competing interpretation.** 

**Applicability to Unbound.** Adapter design.

**Business assumptions.**

- Banks vary.

**Recommended answer.** Support BAI2/BTRS, camt.052/053/054 and bank APIs; prior-day mandatory, intraday optional; capacity uses internal reservations, never feed balance alone.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Per-bank availability.

**Consequences if wrong.** Stale capacity.

**Conservative fallback.** Reservation-based capacity.

### XID-BNK-008 — Confirm provisional decision: Do ACH debit blocks on disbursement accounts conflict with any processor model?

**Disposition:** business-policy-decision · **Confidence:** high · **Jurisdiction:** US: bank treasury service agreements; Nacha R29 return · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** Grant disbursement accounts carry an ACH debit block with an allowlist of named company IDs (bank fee and analysis charges only). Card and ACH processors settle to a separate contribution clearing account, where processor debits (chargebacks, fees) are allowed for the processor's company ID.

**Relevant requirements.** XID-BNK-008 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv), UNB-XIN-BNK-003 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl)

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Debit-blocked accounts return unallowlisted ACH debits (R29); payment providers that fund payouts by ACH debit require their Company IDs to be allowlisted. [EV-C-035, help article (search)]
- *established* — R29 is classified as an unauthorized return. [EV-C-005, Returns]

**Supporting interpretation.** Account separation makes debit blocks compatible with card/ACH processors. The real conflict is with disbursement rails that pull funding by ACH debit from the disbursement account (e.g., bill-pay or grant-rail partners funding by debit); these need their Company IDs allowlisted or a push-funded (wire/book transfer) model. The sponsor's own ODFI offset for originated credits is an internal posting, not an ACH debit (interpretation; confirm per bank).

**Competing interpretation.** No block (simpler) but leaves the disbursement account exposed to unauthorized debits with only a 2-day R29 window.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Unbound account-role model must carry allowlisted Company IDs per account and flag rail partners requiring debit funding.

**Business assumptions.**

- Banks offer debit block and filter services by Company ID.

**Recommended answer.** Confirm with addition: allowlist on disbursement accounts = bank fees + any grant-rail partner that funds by ACH debit (explicitly approved); prefer push funding for rails. Clearing account allows processor debits. Treasury confirms with bank that ODFI offsets are not blocked.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Bank-specific block mechanics.

**Consequences if wrong.** Failed rail fundings (R29) delaying grants, or unauthorized debits.

**Conservative fallback.** Full block with bank-fee exception; rails funded by wire.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Dedicated rail funding sub-account with debit filter and capped balance: cost = extra account; exposure limited to sub-account balance.

**External authorization required.** Bank debit-block/filter service agreement per sponsor account.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a rail partner requiring debit funding, When enabled, Then its Company ID is on the account's allowlist record with approval evidence.
- Given an R29 on a rail funding, Then an alert names the missing Company ID.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- New rail partner
- Bank change

**Related questions.**

- none

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-BNK-008 authority 'Nacha ... TPS vs TPSP; return timeframes': Authority cited is unrelated to debit blocks. → Cite bank service agreements and R29 practice (EV-C-035, EV-C-005).

### XID-ERP-001 — Confirm provisional decision: Billing basis: average daily balance with daily accrual, or period-end balance?

**Disposition:** business-policy-decision · **Confidence:** high · **Jurisdiction:** Contract (sponsor fund agreement / fee schedule); no statute · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** Fund-level fees are computed daily on the daily balance of the declared FeeBase (actual/365, actual/366 in leap years), but posted once per assessment period (monthly or quarterly per schedule). Capacity = journal balance - reservations - accrued-unposted fees. Segments split at open, close, transfer, schedule change; minimum fee prorated by days.

**Relevant requirements.** XID-ERP-001 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv), UNB-XIN-ERP-001 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl), UNB-RCN-FIN-005, UNB-XIN-EVA-001, RCD-018, /home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-24-adversarial-approval-review.md (UNB-XIN-ERP-001 closing minimum fee: revise)

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Several sponsors publish fees on an average daily balance (NPT, GlobalTrust, Idaho CF). [EV-C-043, prior research rows]
- *established* — Fidelity examples are on an average annual balance; method and frequency not confirmed. [EV-C-042, search snippet]

**Supporting interpretation.** Daily computation supports both average-daily and period-end contracts (period-end is a single-day sample). The basis is a contractual term; both must be supported, versioned per schedule.

**Competing interpretation.** Period-end basis is simpler but creates manipulation and proration problems.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Fee engine design.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsor contracts vary.

**Recommended answer.** Confirm daily computation with periodic posting, but support basis per fee schedule version (average daily, period-end, quarter-average). Per the adversarial review, do not prorate or impose a closing minimum fee unless the schedule says so.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Each sponsor's contract wording.

**Consequences if wrong.** Fee disputes; restatement of fees.

**Conservative fallback.** Follow each sponsor's published schedule literally.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** n/a (configuration choice; no regulatory exposure).

**External authorization required.** Sponsor CFO approval of fee schedule wording.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a schedule with basis=period-end, When computed, Then fee uses the period-end balance only.
- Given basis=average-daily and a leap year, Then actual/366 is applied.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- New sponsor contract

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-ERP-001#Q1**: Contracts differ (several sponsors publish average-daily); support both bases via versioned schedules.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- XID-ERP-001 'minimum fee prorated by days': Conflicts with adversarial review revision (no default proration of minimum fee). → Proration only where the schedule states it.

### XID-ERP-002 — Confirm provisional decision: Tax characterization of donor-paid membership fees to a 501(c)(3) sponsor.

**Disposition:** reasoned-interpretation · **Confidence:** medium · **Jurisdiction:** US federal tax · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** A donor-paid membership or account fee is a payment for services, not a charitable contribution. It is never acknowledged as a gift, is receipted as a service fee, and is booked as program service revenue outside fund ledgers.

**Relevant requirements.** XID-ERP-002 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv), UNB-XIN-ERP-002 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl)

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — A payment in exchange for goods or services is a contribution only to the extent it exceeds FMV and the payer intends the excess. [EV-C-029, 1.170A-1(h)(1),(2)]
- *established* — 'In consideration for' includes services received or expected. [EV-C-029, 1.170A-1(h)(4)(i)]
- *established* — Quid pro quo payments over $75 require a written disclosure; penalty $10 per contribution up to $5,000. [EV-C-028, disclosure; penalty]
- *established* — Membership benefits for annual payment of $75 or less may be disregarded (only for listed benefit types such as admissions/discounts). [EV-C-027, p. 4]
- *interpretation* — Rev. Rul. 67-246 dual-payment principle. [EV-C-031, not fetched]

**Supporting interpretation.** A fee expressly charged for account administration services is consideration for services; with arm's-length pricing there is no excess and nothing is deductible. The $75 membership exception does not fit (administration services are not the listed admission/discount privileges).

**Competing interpretation.** Fees paid from outside the fund cover the sponsor's costs of administering charitable assets it owns, arguably a gift; no IRS guidance resolves this.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Receipting and posting configuration.

**Business assumptions.**

- Fee is labeled and priced as a service fee.

**Recommended answer.** Confirm, and prefer the alternative design: rather than an external 'membership fee', let the donor make an additional contribution to the fund (deductible, acknowledged) and charge the disclosed fee against the fund. If an external fee is offered, receipt it as a service fee with no deductibility statement.

**Remaining uncertainty.** No IRS ruling on externally paid DAF fees.

**Consequences if wrong.** If actually a gift, donors lose a deduction (no penalty to sponsor); if treated as gift wrongly, overstatement and 6115/6701 exposure.

**Conservative fallback.** Service-fee receipt, no deduction representation.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Fund-charged fee with donor top-up contribution: cost none; friction low; exposure eliminated; donor keeps deduction.

**External authorization required.** none

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given an externally paid account fee, When receipted, Then the document states it is a payment for services and contains no contribution acknowledgment language.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- IRS guidance on DAF fees
- Final 4966 regulations addressing fees

**Related questions.**

- **UNB-XIN-ERP-002#Q1**: Not deductible when priced as a service fee at FMV; offer donor top-up contributions instead of external fees.

**Corrections to prior records.**

- none

### XID-EVA-003 — Confirm provisional decision: Who bears intermediary fees on partially returned wires and bank fees on returned payments: the fund, the sponsor operating budget, or the recipient?

**Disposition:** business-policy-decision · **Confidence:** high · **Jurisdiction:** US: UCC 4A; bank fee schedules; sponsor fund agreements · **Batch:** C (Banking and payments)

**Original provisional answer.** Intermediary fees on partially returned wires and bank fees on returned payments are sponsor operating expense by default. They are charged to the fund only if the fee schedule disclosed the pass-through before the grant was recommended. Fees caused by recipient-supplied wrong bank details are still sponsor expense; the sponsor may pursue recovery outside the fund.

**Relevant requirements.** XID-EVA-003 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/decisions.csv), UNB-XIN-EVA-005 (/home/user/unbound/requirements/discovery/2026-11-23-cross-industry/data/gap-requirements.jsonl), PAY-06, RCD-028, UNB-XIN-ECO-018

**Authoritative findings.**

- *established* — Absent sender instruction, receiving banks may not deduct their charges from the amount transferred. [EV-C-017, UCC 4A-302(d)]
- *established* — Misdescribed beneficiary losses fall on the non-bank originator with notice. [EV-C-016, UCC 4A-207(c)]

**Supporting interpretation.** No law dictates fund vs operating allocation; it is a fund-agreement and disclosure question. Domestic Article 4A wires should not suffer deductions; partial returns arise mostly in international wires. Charging undisclosed fees to the fund is inconsistent with the sponsor's fee disclosures (business policy).

**Competing interpretation.** Charge return fees to the fund as a cost of the donor's grant recommendation.

**Applicability to Unbound.** Posting rules and fee-schedule configuration.

**Business assumptions.**

- Sponsor fee schedule governs pass-throughs.

**Recommended answer.** Confirm. Add: send domestic wires with no-deduction instructions; international wires default OUR where available; record fee type and cause.

**Remaining uncertainty.** Sponsor-specific fee schedules.

**Consequences if wrong.** Minor misallocation; donor complaints.

**Conservative fallback.** Sponsor bears all fees.

**Alternative business or architecture design.** Disclose a flat return-handling fee in the schedule: cost recovery vs donor friction.

**External authorization required.** Sponsor CFO adoption of fee schedule.

**Acceptance criteria.**

- Given a returned payment fee, When posted, Then it hits sponsor operating unless the fee schedule version at recommendation time disclosed pass-through.

**Reconsideration triggers.**

- Fee schedule change

**Related questions.**
